The condensed homotopy type of a scheme
Peter J. Haine, Tim Holzschuh, Marcin Lara, Catrin Mair, Louis Martini, Sebastian Wolf

Abstract
We study a condensed version of the étale homotopy type of a scheme, which refines both the usual étale homotopy type of Friedlander–Artin–Mazur and the proétale fundamental group of Bhatt–Scholze. In the first part of this paper, we prove that this condensed homotopy type satisfies descent along integral morphisms and that the expected fiber sequences hold. We also provide explicit computations, for example, for rings of continuous functions. A key ingredient in many of our arguments is a description of the condensed homotopy type using the Galois category of a scheme introduced by Barwick–Glasman–Haine. In the second part, we focus on the fundamental group of the condensed homotopy type in more detail. We show that, unexpectedly, the fundamental group of the condensed homotopy type of the affine line 𝐀 𝐂 1 \mathbf{A}^{1}_{\mathbf{C}} over the complex numbers is nontrivial. Nonetheless, its Noohi completion recovers the proétale fundamental group of Bhatt–Scholze. Moreover, we show that a mild correction—passing to the quasiseparated quotient —fixes most of this group’s quirks. Surprisingly, this quotient is often a topological group.



1
Introduction

1.1
Motivation and overview

Let
X
be a locally topologically noetherian scheme.
In their work on the proétale topology
[
BhattScholzeProetale
, §7]
, Bhatt and Scholze defined a refinement of the étale fundamental group called the
proétale
fundamental group
\mathrm{\pi}^{\proet}_{1}(X)
.
Its profinite completion recovers the usual étale fundamental group; moreover, the proétale and étale fundamental groups coincide for normal schemes.
While the étale fundamental group classifies local systems with values in profinite rings such as
\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}
, it generally does not classify
\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}
-local systems.
The proétale fundamental group fixes this, as it has the better feature that it classifies local systems in a more general class of topological rings.

The (SGA 3) étale fundamental group is the fundamental group of the
étale homotopy type
, a proanima introduced by Artin–Mazur
[
MR0245577
, §9]
and Friedlander
[
Fri82
, §4]
.
The étale homotopy type classifies derived
\mathbf{Z}_{\ell}
-local systems, and has a number of important applications.
For example, Friedlander’s
[
Fri73
]
and Sullivan’s
[
Sul74
]
proofs of the Adams Conjecture, Feng’s proof
[
Fen20
]
of Tate’s 1966 conjecture on the Artin–Tate pairing
[
Tat95
]
, and applications to anabelian geometry
[
HSS14
,
SS16
]
.

Motivated by the utility of the proétale fundamental group and the étale homotopy type, one desires a common refinement of the two to a ‘homotopy type’ that classifies derived
\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}
-local systems and refines the key properties of the étale homotopy type.
The main goal of this article is to use the theory of condensed mathematics introduced by Clausen–Scholze
[
Sch19a
]
to investigate such a refinement.

This article is not the first to
introduce
a condensed refinement of the étale homotopy type; one definition has been given by Barwick–Glasman–Haine via exodromy
[
BGH20
, 13.8.10]
, and another one, following a suggestion by Bhatt–Scholze
[
BhattScholzeProetale
, Remark 4.2.9]
, was given by Hemo–Richarz–Scholbach
[
hemo2023constructible_sheaves_schemes
, Appendix A]
.
But beyond a few basic properties, little more was known about these refinements.
Hence, the primary aim of this article is to undertake a thorough investigation of them.

The definition given in
[
hemo2023constructible_sheaves_schemes
]
proceeds as follows.
For a qcqs scheme
X
, pick a proétale hypercover
X_{\bullet}\to X
by w-contractible schemes.
Then for every
n\smallin\mathbf{N}
, the set of connected components
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X_{n})
is naturally a profinite set.
Define the
condensed homotopy type
of
X
to be the colimit

\textstyle\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)\coloneq\colim_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X_{\bullet})\smallin\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}

computed in the
\infty
-category
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
of condensed anima.
The idea is that the condensed homotopy type should be ‘trivial’ (meaning having no higher homotopy groups) on w-contractible affines, and on general schemes, defined via proétale hyperdescent.
More formally,
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}
is the unique hypercomplete proétale cosheaf whose value on w-contractible affines is
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}
.

This definition is convenient for some formal manipulations but often too inexplicit to directly compute in concrete examples.
To remedy this, one of the main tools that we use relies on the work of Barwick–Glasman–Haine
[
BGH20
]
.
They introduced an explicit profinite category
\mathrm{Gal}(X)
whose underlying category is the category of points of the étale topos of
X
; the profinite structure globalizes the topologies on the absolute Galois groups of the residue fields of
X
.

The pro-category
\mathrm{Gal}(X)
can be regarded as a condensed category; the aforementioned condensed refinement of the étale homotopy type proposed by Barwick–Glasman–Haine
[
BGH20
, 13.8.10]
is the
condensed classifying anima
of
\mathrm{Gal}(X)
, obtained by inverting all morphisms in this condensed category.
Wolf showed that the whole hypercomplete proétale
\infty
-topos can be recovered from the condensed category
\mathrm{Gal}(X)

[
Wol22
]
.
Using Wolf’s theorem, we prove in
Proposition

3.38
that this proposed definition agrees with the other proposal mentioned above:

\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)\simeq\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}\mathrm{Gal}(X)\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Before explaining our main results in detail, we now turn to briefly summarizing the contents of this article.
This article consists of two parts.
In the first part, we show that, in many respects, the condensed homotopy type behaves as one would expect from a refinement of the étale homotopy type.
Among other results, we show that an analogue of the
fundamental fiber sequence
holds and that the condensed homotopy type satisfies
integral descent
; see
Theorems

1.1
and
1.3
below.
We also provide explicit computations of the condensed homotopy type, for example for rings of continuous functions
\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{C})
, where
T
is a compact Hausdorff space (see
Theorem

1.4
).

In the second part of this article, we focus on the
condensed fundamental group
.
Every geometric point
\bar{x}\to X
defines a point of the condensed anima
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)
, giving rise to condensed groups

\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{n}(X,\bar{x})\coloneq\mathrm{\pi}_{n}(\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X),\bar{x})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Computing these groups is generally difficult, and the results can be wild and unexpected.
For instance, we prove in
Corollary

7.8
that the fundamental group of the affine line over the complex numbers is
nontrivial
:

\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{1}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{1},\bar{x})\neq 1\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

While this departs from the classical situation, we show that the
Noohi completion
of
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{1}(X,\bar{x})
recovers the proétale fundamental group of Bhatt–Scholze; see
Theorem

8.17
.
In fact, we prove that already the
quasiseparated quotient

\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond},\qs}_{1}(X,\bar{x})
, a milder completion similar to the Hausdorff quotient of topological groups, behaves computationally as expected.
Also, surprisingly, in many situations the quasiseparated quotient turns out to be a topological group.
See
Theorem

1.10
, the van Kampen formula (
Theorem

1.12
), and the Künneth formula (
Theorem

1.13
).
Studying
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond},\qs}_{1}
is another major theme of the second part of this article.

1.2
Results about the condensed homotopy type

We now turn to explaining the results that we prove in the first part of this paper in detail.
The first is a condensed version of the ‘fundamental exact sequence’ for the étale fundamental group.

1.1 Theorem

((fundamental fiber sequence,
Corollary

5.6
))
.

Let
f\colon{X}\to{S}
be a morphism between qcqs schemes, and let
{\bar{s}}\to{S}
be a geometric point of
S
.
If
\dim(S)=0
, then the naturally null sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X_{\bar{s}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(S)}

is a fiber sequence in the
\infty
-category
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
.

Second, using a profinite version of Quillen’s Theorem B, we prove the following analogue of a result of Friedlander
[
Fri73a
, Theorem 3.7]
.

1.2 Theorem

((
Theorem

5.12
))
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a smooth and proper morphism between qcqs schemes and let
\bar{s}\to S
be a geometric point.
Let
\Sigma
be a nonempty set of primes invertible on
S
.
Then the induced map

\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X_{\bar{s}})\to\textup{fib}_{\bar{s}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)\to\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(S))

becomes an equivalence after completion at
\Sigma
.

Third, we show that the hypercomplete proétale
\infty
-topos and the condensed homotopy type have descent along hypercovers by integral surjections:

1.3 Theorem

((integral hyperdescent,
Corollary

6.16
))
.

The functor
X\mapsto X_{\proet}^{\hyp}
sending a qcqs scheme
X
to its hypercomplete proétale
\infty
-topos satisfies integral hyperdescent.
As a consequence, if
X_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X
is an integral hypercover, then the natural map of condensed anima

\textstyle\colim_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X_{\bullet})\to\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)

is an equivalence.

The description of
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)
via exodromy is a crucial ingredient in our proof of
Theorem

1.3
; it follows rather quickly from the fact that, for an integral morphism of schemes
f\colon X\to Y
, the functor

\mathrm{Gal}(f)
is a right fibration of condensed
\infty
-categories.
See
Proposition

6.9
.

Finally, we give a complete computation of the condensed and étale homotopy types of rings of continuous functions to the complex numbers:

1.4 Theorem

((
Corollary

4.35
))
.

Let
T
be a compact Hausdorff space and consider the ring
\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{C})
of continuous functions to the complex numbers.
Then there is a natural equivalence of condensed anima

\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathrm{Spec}(\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{C})))\simeq T\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

(Here, the right-hand side denotes the condensed set represented by
T
.)

As a consequence, up to protruncation, the étale homotopy type of
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{C}))
is equivalent to the shape of the topological space
T
.
In particular, if
T
admits a CW structure, then, up to protruncation, the étale homotopy type of
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{C}))
recovers the underlying anima of
T
.

1.5 Remark
.

The computation of the protruncated étale homotopy type of rings of continuous functions seems new.
We also do not know of a direct computation that does not pass through the condensed homotopy type.

1.3
Results about the condensed fundamental group

We now turn to our results about the condensed fundamental group.
But first, let us remark that we also obtain a reasonably explicit description of the condensed set of connected components of
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)
.

1.6 Theorem

((
Theorems

4.18
and
4.19
))
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme.
Then, for any extremally disconnected profinite set
S
, we have

\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)(S)=\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S,\lvert X\rvert)/\kern-2.34993pt\sim\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}

where
\sim
is the equivalence relation generated by pointwise specializations.

In particular, if
X
has finitely many irreducible components, then
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)
coincides with the usual profinite set
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)
of connected components of
X
.

1.7 Remark

((see
Example

4.24
))
.

Let
R
be a ring with the property that
|\mathrm{Spec}(R)|
is homeomorphic to the underlying spectral space of Huber’s adic unit disk over
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
.
Then the condensed set
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(\mathrm{Spec}(R))
coincides with the
separated quotient
of the space
|\mathrm{Spec}(R)|
.
This is a compact Hausdorff space, and moreover, it coincides with the Berkovich unit disk, i.e.,

\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(\mathrm{Spec}(R))\simeq|\mathbf{D}^{1,\mathrm{Berk}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}|\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

While this example feels rather contrived in the realm of schemes, in a follow-up article we plan to study a similarly defined condensed homotopy type for rigid spaces.

We now turn to our results about the condensed fundamental group.
As stated earlier, the condensed fundamental group of
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{1}
is nontrivial:

1.8 Theorem

((
Corollary

7.8
))
.

Let
\bar{x}\to\mathbf{A}^{1}_{\mathbf{C}}
be a geometric point.
Then the abelianization of the underlying group
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{1}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{1},\bar{x})({\mathbin{*}})
is nontrivial.
As a consequence,
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{1}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{1},\bar{x})\neq 1
.

One way to remedy this lies in the relationship between the condensed and proétale fundamental groups.
The proétale fundamental group has the property that it is a
Noohi group
in the sense of
[
BS15
, §7.1]
.
A consequence of
Theorem

1.8
is that the condensed fundamental group is not generally a Noohi group.
The process of Noohi completion
G\mapsto G^{{\operatorname{Noohi}}}
extends from topological groups to condensed groups, and we prove:

1.9 Theorem

((
Theorem

8.17
))
.

Let
X
be be a qcqs scheme with finitely many irreducible components and
\bar{x}\to X
a geometric point.
Then there is a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{1}(X,\bar{x})^{{\operatorname{Noohi}}}\similarrightarrow\mathrm{\pi}^{\proet}_{1}(X,\bar{x})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

In the case of
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{1}
, we prove that an operation much milder than Noohi completion forces
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{1}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{1})
to become trivial.
Specifically, Clausen and Scholze introduced a localization
{A}\mapsto{A^{\qs}}
of the category of condensed sets called the
quasiseparated quotient

[
Sch19
, Lecture VI]
, and we show:

1.10 Theorem

((
Theorem

7.27
))
.

Let
X
be a qcqs geometrically unibranch scheme with finitely many irreducible components, and let
\bar{x}\to X
be a geometric point.
Then there is a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond},\qs}_{1}(X,\bar{x})\similarrightarrow\mathrm{\pi}_{1}^{\et}(X,\bar{x})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

As a consequence of
Theorems

1.1
and
1.6
, we deduce a fundamental exact sequence for the quasiseparated quotient of the condensed fundamental group:

1.11 Theorem

((fundamental exact sequence,
Corollary

7.26
))
.

Let
k
be a field with separable closure
\bar{k}
, let
X
be a qcqs
k
-scheme, and fix a geometric point
\bar{x}\to X_{\bar{k}}
.
If
X
is geometrically connected and
X_{\bar{k}}
has finitely many irreducible components, then the sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond},\qs}_{1}(X_{\bar{k}},\bar{x})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond},\qs}_{1}(X,\bar{x})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}

is exact.

Theorem

1.10
can be used, together with integral descent (
Theorem

1.3
), to show that for many non-normal schemes, the quasiseparated quotient of the condensed fundamental group still admits a description in terms of the étale fundamental group.
Moreover, surprisingly, it is a (Hausdorff) topological group rather than some more complicated condensed group.

1.12 Theorem

((van Kampen formula for
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond},\qs}_{1}
, special case of
Theorem

7.51
))
.

Let
X
be a Nagata qcqs scheme and let
X^{\nu}=\coprod_{i}X^{\nu}_{i}
be the decomposition of its normalization into connected components.
After choosing base points and étale paths, one has that

\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond},\qs}_{1}(X,\bar{x})\simeq\underline{\big(*^{\mathrm{top}}_{i}\mathrm{\pi}^{\et}_{1}(X^{\nu}_{i},\bar{x}_{i})*^{\mathrm{top}}\mathbf{Z}^{*r}\big)/H^{\prime}}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Here,
\mathbf{Z}^{*r}
is a free (discrete) group of finite rank,
*^{\mathrm{top}}
denotes the free topological product and
H^{\prime}
is an explicit closed normal subgroup.

Using the van Kampen and the Künneth formulas for the étale fundamental group, we prove:

1.13 Theorem

((Künneth formula for
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond},\qs}_{1}
,
Corollary

7.53
))
.

Let
k
be a separably closed field and let
X
and
Y
be schemes of finite type over
k
.
If
Y
is proper or
\characteristic(k)=0
, then the natural homomorphism of condensed groups

\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond},\qs}_{1}(X\times_{k}Y,(\bar{x},\bar{y}))\to\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond},\qs}_{1}(X,\bar{x})\times\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond},\qs}_{1}(Y,\bar{y})

is an isomorphism.

In some ways, the group
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond},\qs}_{1}
is even better-behaved than
\mathrm{\pi}^{\proet}_{1}
(see, e.g.,
Remark

7.56
).

1.4
Related work

As mentioned earlier, the first definitions of the condensed homotopy type were given via exodromy by Barwick–Glasman–Haine
[
BGH20
, 13.8.10]
, by Bhatt–Scholze
[
BhattScholzeProetale
, Remark 4.2.9]
and by Hemo–Richarz–Scholbach
[
hemo2023constructible_sheaves_schemes
, Appendix A]
.
Another approach to the condensed homotopy type that mostly uses (simplicial) topological spaces rather than condensed mathematics (along the lines of Artin and Mazur’s work) was studied by Meffle
[
Meffle:proetalehomotopytype
]
.

Some results and definitions in this article constitute a part of doctoral theses of the forth
[
Mai25
]
and sixth
[
Wol25
]
named authors.

1.5
Linear overview

In
Section

2
, we recall some preliminaries on condensed anima, pro-objects, condensed
\infty
-categories, and proétale sheaves.

Part

I
is dedicated to proving fundamental results about the condensed homotopy type.
In
Section

3
, we give three definitions of the condensed homotopy type, and prove that they are equivalent.
We also compute the condensed homotopy type of henselian local rings (
Corollary

3.48
).
In
Section

4
, we prove
Theorem

1.6
, giving an explicit description of the connected components of the condensed homotopy type.
As an application of this explicit description, we also we compute the condensed homotopy type of rings of continuous functions (
Theorem

1.4
).

Section

5
is dedicated to producing fiber sequences for the condensed homotopy type.
Specifically, we prove the fundamental fiber sequence (
Theorem

1.1
) as well as an analogue of a result of Friedlander relating the condensed homotopy type of the geometric fiber of a smooth proper morphism to the fiber of the induced map on condensed homotopy types (
Theorem

1.2
).
In
Section

6
, we prove that the condensed homotopy type satisfies integral hyperdescent (
Theorem

1.3
).

In
Part

II
, we turn our attention to the condensed fundamental group.
In
Section

7
, we start by showing that
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{1}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{1})
is nontrivial (
Theorem

1.8
).
We then study the quasiseparated quotient of the condensed fundamental group.
In particular, we prove
Theorems

1.10
,
1.11
,
1.12
, and
1.13
.
In
Section

8
, we prove that the Noohi completion of the condensed fundamental group recovers the proétale fundamental group (
Theorem

1.9
).

We have three appendices.

Appendix

A
, by Bogdan Zavyalov, is on the structure of rings of continuous functions and the relationship between these rings and Čech–Stone compactification.
We need these results for the computation of the condensed homotopy type of rings of continuous functions, however were not able to find any sources that contained all of the results we needed.

In
Appendix

B
, we prove an analogue of Quillen’s Theorem B for profinite completions of classifying anima of condensed
\infty
-categories.
Together with the description of the condensed homotopy type via exodromy, this is the key tool we use to prove
Theorem

1.2
.

It is well-known that there is an isomorphism between the absolute Galois group of the function field
\mathbf{C}(t)
and the free profinite group on the set
\mathbf{C}
.
See, for example
[
Dou64
,
HJ00
,
Jar95
]
.
It seems to be folklore that this isomorphism can be chosen to be compatible with decomposition groups; this is crucial for our proof that
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{1}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{1})\neq 1
.
Since we could not find this proven in the literature, and there are some subtleties involved, we have included a proof in
Appendix

C
.

1.6
Conventions

Set theory

As usual when working with condensed mathematics, there are some set-theoretic issues one needs to deal with.
We give detailed explanations on how we handle these in
Remarks

2.4
,
2.36
, and
3.18
.

Notational conventions

We use the following standard notation.

(1)

We write
\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty}
for the large
\infty
-category of small
\infty
-categories, and write
\textbf{{Ani}}\subset\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty}
for the full subcategory spanned by the anima (also called
\infty
-groupoids or spaces).

(2)

Given a small
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
, we write
\PSh(\mathcal{C})\coloneq\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\op},\textbf{{Ani}})
for the
\infty
-category of presheaves of anima on
\mathcal{C}
.

(3)

Given an
\infty
-topos
\mathcal{X}
, we write
\mathcal{X}^{\hyp}\subset\mathcal{X}
for the full subcategory spanned by the hypercomplete objects.
The inclusion is accessible and admits a left exact accessible left adjoint, so that
\mathcal{X}^{\hyp}
is also an
\infty
-topos, called the
hypercompletion
of
\mathcal{X}
.

(4)

Given an
\infty
-site
(\mathcal{C},\tau)
, we write
\Sh_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})
for the
\infty
-topos of sheaves of anima on
\mathcal{C}
with respect to
\tau
.
We write
\Sh^{\hyp}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})\coloneq\Sh_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})^{\hyp}
.
The
\infty
-topos
\Sh^{\hyp}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})
can also be identified as the full subcategory of
\Sh_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})
spanned by those sheaves that also satisfy descent for
hypercovers
.
If the topology
\tau
is clear from the context, we may omit it from the notation.

(5)

Given a scheme
X
, we write
\mathrm{\acute{E}t}_{X}
and
\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}_{X}
for its
étale
and
proétale site
, respectively.
Moreover, we write
X_{\et}\coloneq\Sh(\mathrm{\acute{E}t}_{X})
and
X_{\proet}\coloneq\Sh(\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}_{X})
for the
\infty
-topoi of étale and proétale sheaves of anima on
X
, respectively.

(6)

For an integer
n\geq 0
, we write
[n]
for the poset
\{0<\cdots<n\}
.

(7)

For each integer
n\geq 0
, we write
\mathbf{\Delta}_{\leq n}\subset\mathbf{\Delta}
for the full subcategory spanned by
[0]
, …,
[n]
.

2
Preliminaries

For later use and the convenience of the reader, in this section we record a few definitions and observations on condensed anima (
Section

2.1
), pro-anima and their relation to condensed anima (
Section

2.2
), condensed
\infty
-categories (
Section

2.3
), shape theory (
Section

2.4
), and proétale sheaves and w-contractible objects (
Section

2.5
).

2.1
Recollection on condensed anima

All of the material contained in this subsection is gathered from
[
BH19
]
and
[
Sch19a
]
.

2.1 Notation
.

We write
Top
for the category of topological spaces, and
\textbf{{Comp}}\subset\textbf{{Top}}
for the full subcategory spanned by the compact Hausdorff spaces.
We write
\mathrm{\beta}\colon{\textbf{{Top}}}\to{\textbf{{Comp}}}
for the Čech–Stone compactification functor, i.e., the left adjoint to the inclusion.
By Stone duality, the category
\Pro(\textbf{{Set}}_{\textup{fin}})
of profinite sets embeds fully faithfully into
Comp
with image the full subcategory spanned by the totally disconnected compact Hausdorff spaces.
We write

\textbf{{Extr}}\subset\Pro(\textbf{{Set}}_{\textup{fin}})

for the full subcategory spanned by the
extremally disconnected
profinite sets.
By a theorem of Gleason
[
MR0121775
]
, the projective objects of the category
Comp
are exactly the extremally disconnected profinite sets.
Moreover, a profinite set is extremally disconnected if and only if it is a retract of the Čech–Stone compactification of a set equipped with the discrete topology.

2.2 Recollection

((condensed anima))
.

Give the category
Comp
of compact Hausdorff spaces the Grothendieck topology where the covering families are generated by finite jointly surjective families.
For each compact Hausdorff space
T
, let
T^{\mathrm{\delta}}
denote the underlying set of
T
equipped with the discrete topology.
By the universal property of Čech–Stone compactification the ‘identity’ map
{T^{\mathrm{\delta}}}\to{T}
extends to a surjection
{\mathrm{\beta}(T^{\mathrm{\delta}})}\twoheadrightarrow{T}
.
In particular, every compact Hausdorff space admits a surjection from an extremally disconnected profinite set.
Hence the subcategories

\textbf{{Extr}}\subset\Pro(\textbf{{Set}}_{\textup{fin}})\subset\textbf{{Comp}}

are bases for the topology of finite jointly surjective families.
By
[
arXiv:2001.00319
, Corollary A.7]
, the restriction functors define equivalences of hypercomplete
\infty
-topoi

(2.3)

\Sh^{\hyp}(\textbf{{Comp}})\similarrightarrow\Sh^{\hyp}(\Pro(\textbf{{Set}}_{\textup{fin}}))\similarrightarrow\Sh^{\hyp}(\textbf{{Extr}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

The
\infty
-topos
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
of
condensed anima
is any of the equivalent
\infty
-topoi (
2.3
).

Since every surjection
T^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow T
of profinite sets with
T
extremally disconnected admits a section, a presheaf
F
on
Extr
is a hypersheaf if and only if
F
carries finite disjoint unions to finite products.
That is,

\Sh^{\hyp}(\textbf{{Extr}})\simeq\mathrm{Fun}^{\times}(\textbf{{Extr}}^{\op},\textbf{{Ani}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

From this description it follows that sifted colimits in
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
can be computed in the presheaf category
\mathrm{Fun}(\textbf{{Extr}}^{\op},\textbf{{Ani}})
.

2.4 Remark
.

Since the category
Comp
of compact Hausdorff spaces is not a small category, there are some set-theoretic issues in the above discussion.
We explain how to deal with these issues in
Remark

2.36
.

Given the final description of condensed anima, we make the following convenient general definition.

2.5 Definition

((condensed objects))
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an
\infty
-category with finite products.
The
\infty
-category of
condensed objects
of
\mathcal{C}
is the
\infty
-category

\Cond(\mathcal{C})\coloneq\mathrm{Fun}^{\times}(\textbf{{Extr}}^{\op},\mathcal{C})

of finite product-preserving presheaves
{\textbf{{Extr}}^{\op}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
.
If
\mathcal{D}
is another
\infty
-category with finite products and
F\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
is a finite product-preserving functor, we write

F^{\mathrm{cond}}\colon{\Cond(\mathcal{C})}\to{\Cond(\mathcal{D})}

for the functor given by post-composition with
F
.

2.6
.

Observe that if
F\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
admits a right adjoint
G
, then
G^{\mathrm{cond}}
is right adjoint to
F^{\mathrm{cond}}
.

2.7 Recollection

((homotopy groups of condensed anima))
.

The functor
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}\colon{\textbf{{Ani}}}\to{\textbf{{Set}}}
preserves finite products.
Moreover, for each integer
n\geq 1
, the functor
\mathrm{\pi}_{n}\colon{\textbf{{Ani}}_{*}}\to{\textbf{{Grp}}}
preserves finite products.
There is a canonical identification

\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})_{*}=\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}}_{*})

between pointed objects of condensed anima and condensed objects of pointed anima.
We simply write
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}\colon{\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}\to{\Cond(\textbf{{Set}})}
for
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}
and
\mathrm{\pi}_{n}\colon{\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})_{*}}\to{\Cond(\textbf{{Grp}})}
for

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})_{*}=\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}}_{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cond(\textbf{{Grp}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{n}}

Explicitly, given a condensed anima
A
, the condensed set
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(A)\colon{\textbf{{Extr}}^{\op}}\to{\textbf{{Set}}}
is given by

\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(A)(S)\coloneq\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(A(S))\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Similarly, given a global section
a\colon{{\mathbin{*}}}\to{A}
, the condensed group
\mathrm{\pi}_{n}(A,a)
is given by

\mathrm{\pi}_{n}(A,a)(S)\coloneq\mathrm{\pi}_{n}(A(S),a)\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

2.8 Recollection

(
[
BH19
, Construction 2.2.12]
)
.

Write

\ev_{\mathbin{*}}\colon\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})\to\textbf{{Ani}}

for the global sections functor, given by
A\mapsto A({\mathbin{*}})
.
The functor
\ev_{{\mathbin{*}}}
admits a left adjoint, that we denote by

(-)^{\disc}\colon\textbf{{Ani}}\to\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})

Furthermore
(-)^{\disc}
is fully faithful.
We call the image of
(-)^{\disc}
the
discrete
condensed anima.

2.9 Recollection

((the restricted Yoneda embedding))
.

The restricted Yoneda embedding defines a functor

Top

\displaystyle\to\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}\hskip 11.74988pt{T}\mapsto{\underline{T}}

given by

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle\mapsto[S\mapsto\mathrm{Map}_{\textbf{{Top}}}(S,T)]\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Note that this functor factors through
\Cond(\textbf{{Set}})\subset\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
.
1
1

1

However, note that if
T
is not
\mathrm{T}_{1}
, then the the sheaf
\mathrm{Map}_{\textbf{{Top}}}(-,T)
is not generally
accessible

[
Sch19a
, Warning 2.14 & Proposition 2.15]
.
So, depending on which way you deal with set-theoretic issues, it is not a condensed set, cf.
Remark

2.36
.
However, in this paper, we only apply this functor to
\mathrm{T}_{1}
topological spaces anyways.

Also recall that this functor is fully faithful when restricted to the full subcategory of
Top
spanned by the compactly generated topological spaces
[
Sch19a
, Proposition 1.7]
.
Since it rarely leads to confusion, we often omit the underline and simply write
T
for
\underline{T}
.

2.2
Pro-objects and completions

We now turn to some recollections about proanima and their relation to condensed anima.

2.10 Recollection

((
\mathrm{\pi}
-finite and truncated anima))
.

Let
A
be an anima.

(1)

We say that
A
is
truncated
if there exists an integer
n\geq 0
such that for all
a\smallin A
and
k\geq n
, we have
\mathrm{\pi}_{k}(A,a)=0
.

(2)

We say that
A
is
\mathrm{\pi}
-finite
if
A
is truncated,
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(A)
is finite, and for all
a\smallin A
and
k>0
, the group
\mathrm{\pi}_{k}(A,a)
is finite.

(3)

We write
\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}}\subset\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty}\subset\textbf{{Ani}}
for the full subcategories of
Ani
spanned by the
\mathrm{\pi}
-finite and truncated anima, respectively.

2.11 Recollection

((on various completions))
.

(1)

Since
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
admits cofiltered limits, the inclusions

\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}}\subset\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty}\subset\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})

extend to cofiltered-limit-preserving functors

\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}})\hookrightarrow\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty})\to\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Here, the functor
\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty})\to\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
is
not
fully faithful.
However, by
[
BH19
,
Hai25
]
, its restriction to
\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}})
is fully faithful.

(2)

The above chain of functors
\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}})\hookrightarrow\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty})\to\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
admits left adjoints

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\wedge}_{\disc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{\mathrm{\pi}}^{\wedge}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{\mathrm{\pi}}^{\wedge}}

that we call the
prodiscretization
, resp.,
profinite completion
functors.

(3)

Similarly, the inclusions
\FinSet\subset\Cond(\textbf{{Set}})
and
\FinGrp\subset\Cond(\textbf{{Grp}})
induce inclusions
\ProFin\subset\Cond(\textbf{{Set}})
and
\ProFinGrp\subset\Cond(\textbf{{Grp}})
that admit left adjoints

\Cond(\textbf{{Set}})\to\ProFin\text{\hskip 23.49976ptand\hskip 23.49976pt}(-)^{\wedge}\colon\Cond(\textbf{{Grp}})\to\ProFinGrp

that we refer to as
profinite completion
functors.

We now explain the effect of profintie completion of condensed anima on
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}
and
\mathrm{\pi}_{1}
.

2.12 Lemma

((completions &
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}/\mathrm{\pi}_{1}
))
.

Let
A
be a condensed anima and
a\colon{\mathbin{*}}\to A
a point.

(1)

The map
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(A)\to\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(A_{\mathrm{\pi}}^{\wedge})
induced by the unit map
A\to A_{\mathrm{\pi}}^{\wedge}
exhibits
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(A_{\mathrm{\pi}}^{\wedge})
as the profinite completion of
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(A)
.

(2)

If
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(A)\smallin\Cond(\textbf{{Set}})
is discrete, then the unit map
A\to A_{\mathrm{\pi}}^{\wedge}
induces an isomorphism of profinite groups

\mathrm{\pi}_{1}(A,a)^{\wedge}\similarrightarrow\mathrm{\pi}_{1}(A_{\mathrm{\pi}}^{\wedge},a)\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Proof.

For (
1
), note that since the square of inclusions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pro(\textbf{{Set}}_{\textup{fin}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cond(\textbf{{Set}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}

commutes, so does the induced square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cond(\textbf{{Set}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ProFin}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{\mathrm{\pi}}^{\wedge}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\pi}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\pi}_{0}}

of left adjoints.

For (
2
), since
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(A)
is a set, we may assume that
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(A)={\mathbin{*}}
.
It suffices to show that, for any finite group
G
, precomposition induces a bijection

\mathrm{Map}_{\Cond(\textbf{{Grp}})}(\mathrm{\pi}_{1}(A,a),G)\similarrightarrow\mathrm{Map}_{\Cond(\textbf{{Grp}})}(\mathrm{\pi}_{1}(A_{\mathrm{\pi}}^{\wedge},a),G)=\mathrm{Map}_{\ProFinGrp}(\mathrm{\pi}_{1}(A_{\mathrm{\pi}}^{\wedge},a),G)\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

To see this, note that we have a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\pi}_{0}\mathrm{Map}_{\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}})_{\mathbin{*}}}(A_{\mathrm{\pi}}^{\wedge},\mathrm{B}G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Map}_{\ProFinGrp}(\mathrm{\pi}_{1}(A_{\mathrm{\pi}}^{\wedge},a),G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\pi}_{0}\mathrm{Map}_{\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})_{\mathbin{*}}}(A,\mathrm{B}G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Map}_{\Cond(\textbf{{Grp}})}(\mathrm{\pi}_{1}(A,a),G),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\pi}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\pi}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where the vertical maps are those induced by the unit transformation
A\to A_{\mathrm{\pi}}^{\wedge}
.
Since
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(A)={\mathbin{*}}
, by the equivalence of
1
-truncated, pointed connected objects and group objects
[
HTT
,
Theorem 7.2.2.12
]
, the horizontal maps are bijections.
It thus suffices to see that the map

\mathrm{Map}_{\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})_{\mathbin{*}}}(A_{\mathrm{\pi}}^{\wedge},\mathrm{B}G)\to\mathrm{Map}_{\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})_{\mathbin{*}}}(A,\mathrm{B}G)

induces a bijection on
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}
.
But since
G
is finite and
\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}})_{\mathbin{*}}\hookrightarrow\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})_{\mathbin{*}}
is fully faithful, by adjunction it is even an equivalence.
∎

2.13 Remark
.

One cannot drop the assumption that
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(A)
is discrete in
Lemma

2.12
(
2
).
Indeed, let
A
be the condensed
set
represented by the topological circle
\mathrm{S}^{1}
.
Then for any
x\smallin\mathrm{S}^{1}
, we have

\mathrm{\pi}_{1}(A,x)={\mathbin{*}}\hskip 11.74988pt\textup{ but }\hskip 11.74988pt\mathrm{\pi}_{1}(A_{\mathrm{\pi}}^{\wedge},x)=\widehat{\mathbf{Z}}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

2.3
Condensed
\infty
-categories

We now recall some background on internal higher category theory and condensed
\infty
-categories.
The main point is that it is often useful to use the fact that the
\infty
-category of condensed
\infty
-categories is equivalent to the
\infty
-category of categories internal to condensed anima.
We refer the reader to
[
Mar21
,
MW24
]
for more background about internal higher category theory.

2.14 Definition
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be an
\infty
-category with finite limits.
A
category internal to
\mathcal{B}
is a simplicial object
F\colon{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

Segal condition:
For each integer
n\geq 2
, the natural map

F([n])\to F(\{0<1\})\operatornamewithlimits{\times}_{F(\{1\})}F(\{1<2\})\operatornamewithlimits{\times}_{F(\{2\})}\cdots\operatornamewithlimits{\times}_{F(\{n-1\})}F(\{n-1<n\})

is an equivalence in
\mathcal{B}
.

(2)

Univalence axiom:
The natural square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F([0])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F([0])\times F([0])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F([3])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\{0<2\})\times F(\{1<3\})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}

is a pullback square in
\mathcal{B}
.
Here, the left vertical map is given by restriction along the unique map
[3]\to[0]
, the right vertical map is the product of the maps given by restriction along the unique maps
\{0<2\}\to[0]
and
\{1<3\}\to[0]
, and the bottom horizontal map is induced by restriction along the inclusions
\{0<2\}\hookrightarrow[3]
and
\{1<3\}\hookrightarrow[3]
.

We write

\mathrm{Cat}(\mathcal{B})\subset\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\mathcal{B})

for the full subcategory spanned by the categories internal to
\mathcal{B}
.

2.15 Remark
.

Elsewhere in the literature, internal categories are also called
complete Segal objects
.

2.16
.

Joyal and Tierney
[
JT07
]
showed that the nerve construction defines an equivalence

\displaystyle\Nerve\colon\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\similarrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}(\textbf{{Ani}})

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle\mapsto[[n]\mapsto\mathrm{Map}_{\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty}}([n],C)]

from the
\infty
-category of
\infty
-categories to the
\infty
-category of categories internal to anima.
See
[
HS25
]
for a modern, model-independent proof of this fact.

2.17
.

The main example that we care about in this paper is the case where
\mathcal{B}=\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
.
Since the Segal conditions and the sheaf condition are both limit conditions, the canonical equivalence

\mathrm{Fun}(\textbf{{Extr}}^{\op},\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\textbf{{Ani}}))\simeq\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\mathrm{Fun}(\textbf{{Extr}}^{\op},\textbf{{Ani}}))

restricts to an equivalence

\Cond(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})\simeq\mathrm{Cat}(\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}}))\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Therefore, we often implicitly identify
\Cond(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})
with
\mathrm{Cat}(\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}}))
.

We now turn to some specific features of
\Cond(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})
.

2.18 Definition

((continuous functors))
.

The
\infty
-category of condensed
\infty
-categories is cartesian closed, see
[
Mar21
, Proposition 3.2.11]
.
For condensed
\infty
-categories
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
, we denote the internal
\Hom
by

\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Similarly, we write

\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})\coloneq\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})({\mathbin{*}})

for the
\infty
-category of
continuous functors

{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
.

2.19
.

Observe that the functor
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})\mapsto\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})
is characterized by the existence of natural equivalences

\mathrm{Map}_{\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty}}(\mathcal{A},\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}))\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\Cond(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})}(\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})

for each
\infty
-category
\mathcal{A}
.

2.20
.

Explicitly,
\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})
is given by the end

\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})\simeq\int_{S\smallin\textbf{{Extr}}^{\op}}\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{C}(S),\mathcal{D}(S))\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}

see, for example,
[
Gla16
, Proposition 2.3]
.
In particular, the objects in this
\infty
-category are precisely natural transformations
\mathcal{C}(-)\to\mathcal{D}(-)
of functors
\textbf{{Extr}}^{\op}\to\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty}
.

Many of the condensed
\infty
-categories we are interested come from pro-objects:

2.21 Observation
.

By taking internal categories on each side, the right adjoint fully faithful embedding
\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}})\to\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
of
2.11
induces a fully faithful right adjoint functor

\iota\colon\mathrm{Cat}(\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}}))\to\Cond(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Many of the examples of condensed
\infty
-categories that we care about are in the image of this embedding.

For condensed
\infty
-categories in the image of
\iota
, we can describe their value at Čech–Stone compactifations explicitly:

2.22 Proposition
.

Consider
\mathcal{C}\smallin\mathrm{Cat}(\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}}))
as a condensed
\infty
-category via
\iota
and let
M
be a set.
Then the functor

\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{\beta}(M),\mathcal{C})\to\prod_{m\smallin M}\mathcal{C}(\{m\})

induced by the inclusions
{\{m\}}\hookrightarrow{\mathrm{\beta}(M)}
is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories.

Proof.

It suffices to check that this functor becomes an equivalence after applying the functor
\mathrm{Map}_{\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty}}([n],-)
for every
n
.
Since we have a natural chain of equivalences

\displaystyle\mathrm{Map}_{\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty}}([n],\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{\beta}(M),\mathcal{C}))

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\Cond(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})}(\mathrm{\beta}(M)\times[n],\mathcal{C})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\Cond(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})}(\mathrm{\beta}(M),\ev_{[n]}(\mathcal{C})),

it suffices to show that the natural map

\mathrm{Map}_{\Cond(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})}(\mathrm{\beta}(M),\ev_{[n]}(\mathcal{C}))\to\prod_{m\smallin M}\ev_{[n]}(\mathcal{C})(\{m\})

is an equivalence.
Since
\ev_{[n]}(\mathcal{C})
is a profinite anima by assumption and both sides are clearly compatible with limits, we may assume that
\ev_{[n]}(\mathcal{C})=A
is a
\mathrm{\pi}
-finite anima.

By
[
SAG
,
Lemma E.1.6.5
]
, there exists a Kan complex
A_{\bullet}
with values in finite sets such that
\lvert A_{\bullet}\rvert\simeq A
.
Since
\mathrm{\beta}(M)
is a compact projective object in
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
, it follows that the natural map

\lvert\mathrm{Map}_{\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}(\mathrm{\beta}(M),A_{\bullet})\rvert\to\mathrm{Map}_{\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}(\mathrm{\beta}(M),\lvert A_{\bullet}\rvert)

is an equivalence.
Since every
A_{n}
is finite, it follows that
\mathrm{Map}_{\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}(\mathrm{\beta}(M),A_{\bullet})\simeq\prod_{M}A_{\bullet}
is an infinite product of Kan complexes.
Since geometric realizations of Kan complexes commute with arbitrary products,
2
2

2

This follows from the fact that the homotopy groups of the geometric realization of a Kan complex are computed as its simplicial homotopy groups, and these commute with infinite products.

the natural map

\mathrm{Map}_{\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}(\mathrm{\beta}(M),A)\simeq\lvert\mathrm{Map}_{\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}(\mathrm{\beta}(M),A_{\bullet})\rvert\longrightarrow\prod_{M}\lvert A_{\bullet}\rvert\simeq\prod_{M}A

is an equivalence.
∎

2.4
Recollection on shape theory

In this subsection, we recall a bit about shape theory for
\infty
-topoi.
We do not explicitly need shape theory for most of this paper, but, instead, we work with a relative version of shape theory over the base
\infty
-topos of condensed anima.
So this subsection serves as motivation for the theory we develop; we also use it to recall some background on shapes of topological spaces and the étale homotopy type.

2.23 Recollection

((protruncation))
.

The inclusion
\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty})\subset\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}})
admits a left adjoint

\trun_{<\infty}\colon{\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}})}\to{\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty})}

defined as follows.
The functor
\trun_{<\infty}
is the unique cofiltered-limit-preserving extension of the fully faithful functor
{\textbf{{Ani}}}\hookrightarrow{\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty})}
that sends an anima
A
to the cofiltered diagram given by its Postnikov tower
\{\trun_{\leq n}(A)\}_{n\geq 0}
.
We refer to
\trun_{<\infty}
as the
protruncation
functor.

2.24 Recollection
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be an
\infty
-topos.
We write
\Gamma_{{\mathbin{*}}}\coloneq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{X}}(1_{\mathcal{X}},-)\colon\mathcal{X}\to\textbf{{Ani}}
for the
global sections
functor.
The functor
\Gamma_{{\mathbin{*}}}
admits a left exact left adjoint
\Gamma^{{\mathbin{*}}}\colon\textbf{{Ani}}\to\mathcal{X}
referred to as the
constant sheaf
functor.
The
\infty
-topos of anima is the terminal object of
\textbf{{RTop}}_{\infty}
, so
\Gamma_{{\mathbin{*}}}
is the unique geometric morphism
\mathcal{X}\to\textbf{{Ani}}
.

While
\Gamma^{{\mathbin{*}}}
need not preserve limits in general, the unique cofiltered limit-preserving extension
\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}})\to\mathcal{X}
of
\Gamma^{{\mathbin{*}}}
preserves all limits and admits a left adjoint

\Gamma_{\sharp}\colon\mathcal{X}\to\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

2.25 Recollection
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be an
\infty
-topos.
The
shape
of
\mathcal{X}
is the proanima

\Pi_{\infty}(\mathcal{X})\coloneq\Gamma_{\sharp}(1_{\mathcal{X}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

The assignment
\mathcal{X}\mapsto\Pi_{\infty}(\mathcal{X})
naturally refines to a functor

\Pi_{\infty}\colon\textbf{{RTop}}_{\infty}\to\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}})

that is left adjoint to the unique cofiltered limit-preserving extension of the functor

Ani

\displaystyle\to\textbf{{RTop}}_{\infty}

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathrm{Fun}(A,\textbf{{Ani}})\simeq\textbf{{Ani}}_{/A}

with functoriality given by right Kan extension.

The
protruncated shape
functor is the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{<\infty}\colon\textbf{{RTop}}_{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\trun_{<\infty}}

Similarly, the
profinite shape
is defined by composing further with the profinite completion functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Pi}_{\infty}\colon\textbf{{RTop}}_{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{<\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{\mathrm{\pi}}^{\wedge}}

2.26 Observation
.

The prodiscritization functor
(-)^{\wedge}_{\disc}\colon\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})\to\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty})
is the composite of
\Gamma_{\sharp}\colon\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})\to\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}})
with the protruncation functor
\trun_{<\infty}
.

We now give a useful, alternative description of the shape.

2.27 Recollection
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an accessible
\infty
-category with finite limits (e.g.,
\mathcal{C}=\textbf{{Ani}}
).
Then by
[
SAG
,
Definition A.8.1.1
&
Proposition A.8.1.6
]
, there is a natural identification

\Pro(\mathcal{C})\simeq\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{lex},\mathrm{acc}}(\mathcal{C},\textbf{{Ani}})^{\op}

with the opposite of the
\infty
-category of left exact accessible functors
{\mathcal{C}}\to{\textbf{{Ani}}}
.
Under these identifications, the protruncation functor
\trun_{<\infty}\colon\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}})\to\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty})
is identified with the functor

\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{lex},\mathrm{acc}}(\textbf{{Ani}},\textbf{{Ani}})^{\op}\to\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{lex},\mathrm{acc}}(\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty},\textbf{{Ani}})^{\op}

given by precomposition with the inclusion
\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty}\hookrightarrow\textbf{{Ani}}
.

Given an
\infty
-topos
\mathcal{X}
, under this identification of
\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}})
, the shape
\Pi_{\infty}(\mathcal{X})
is the left exact accessible functor
{\textbf{{Ani}}}\to{\textbf{{Ani}}}
given by the composite

\Gamma_{\mathcal{X},{\mathbin{*}}}\Gamma^{{\mathbin{*}}}_{\mathcal{X}}\colon{\textbf{{Ani}}}\to{\textbf{{Ani}}}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

That is, for each anima
A
, the value of
\Pi_{\infty}(\mathcal{X})
on
A
is the global sections of the constant object of
\mathcal{X}
with value
A
.
Moreover, given a geometric morphism
f_{{\mathbin{*}}}\colon{\mathcal{X}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
with unit
u\colon{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}}}\to{f_{{\mathbin{*}}}f^{{\mathbin{*}}}}
, the induced morphism of proanima
{\Pi_{\infty}(\mathcal{X})}\to{\Pi_{\infty}(\mathcal{Y})}
corresponds to the morphism

\Gamma_{\mathcal{Y},{\mathbin{*}}}u\Gamma^{{\mathbin{*}}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\colon\Gamma_{\mathcal{Y},{\mathbin{*}}}\Gamma^{{\mathbin{*}}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\longrightarrow\Gamma_{\mathcal{Y},{\mathbin{*}}}f_{{\mathbin{*}}}f^{{\mathbin{*}}}\Gamma^{{\mathbin{*}}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\simeq\Gamma_{\mathcal{X},{\mathbin{*}}}\Gamma^{{\mathbin{*}}}_{\mathcal{X}}

in
\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}})^{\op}\subset\mathrm{Fun}(\textbf{{Ani}},\textbf{{Ani}})
.
We refer the reader to
[
HTT
,
Hoy18
]
for more details.

We now explain how the shape of the
\infty
-topos of sheaves on a locally compact Hausdorff space
T
relates to the prodiscretization of the condensed set represented by
T
in the sense of
2.11
.
To do this, we first need the following lemma.

2.28 Lemma
.

Let
f_{{\mathbin{*}}}\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
be a geometric morphism of
\infty
-topoi.
If
f^{{\mathbin{*}}}
is fully faithful when restricted to truncated objects, then
\Pi_{<\infty}(f_{{\mathbin{*}}})\colon\Pi_{<\infty}(\mathcal{X})\to\Pi_{<\infty}(\mathcal{Y})
is an equivalence.

Proof.

Note that since
f^{{\mathbin{*}}}
and
f_{{\mathbin{*}}}
are left exact, they preserve truncated objects
[
HTT
,
Proposition 5.5.6.16
]
.
Hence the adjunction
f^{{\mathbin{*}}}\shortlefttack f_{{\mathbin{*}}}
restricts to an adjunction at the level of truncated objects.
Thus our assumption is that the unit
u\colon{\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{Y}}}\to{f_{{\mathbin{*}}}f^{{\mathbin{*}}}}
is an equivalence when restricted to truncated objects.
Under the description of the protruncated shape given in
2.27
, we see that we need to show that for each truncated anima
A
, the map induced by the unit

\Gamma_{\mathcal{Y},{\mathbin{*}}}\Gamma^{{\mathbin{*}}}_{\mathcal{Y}}(A)\longrightarrow\Gamma_{\mathcal{Y},{\mathbin{*}}}f_{{\mathbin{*}}}f^{{\mathbin{*}}}\Gamma^{{\mathbin{*}}}_{\mathcal{Y}}(A)

is an equivalence; this follows from our assumption.
∎

2.29 Example
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be an
\infty
-topos.
There are natural geometric morphisms

\mathcal{X}^{\post}\to\mathcal{X}^{\hyp}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{X}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Here,
\mathcal{X}^{\post}
is the
Postnikov completion
of
\mathcal{X}
in the sense of
[
SAG
,
Definition A.7.2.5
]
.
By
[
SAG
,
Theorem A.7.2.4
]
and
[
HTT
,
Lemma 6.5.2.9
]
, these geometric morphisms restrict to equivalences on truncated objects.
Hence they induce equivalences on protruncated shapes.

Now we deal with sheaves on locally compact Hausdorff spaces.

2.30 Notation
.

For a topological space
T
, we write
\Pi_{\infty}(T)\smallin\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}})
for the shape of the
\infty
-topos
\Sh(T)
of sheaves of anima on
T
.
We write
\Pi_{<\infty}(T)
for the protruncation of
\Pi_{\infty}(T)
.
We write
\textbf{{LCH}}\subset\textbf{{Top}}
for the full subcategory spanned by the locally compact Hausdorff spaces.

2.31 Example
.

If
T
is a topological space that admits a CW structure, then
\Pi_{\infty}(T)
coincides with the underlying anima of
T
.
See
[
HA
,
arXiv:2010.06473
]
.

2.32 Lemma
.

The triangle

LCH
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{<\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\wedge}_{\disc}}

canonically commutes.

Proof.

Let
T
be a locally compact Hausdorff space.
By
[
haine2022descentsheavescompacthausdorff
, Corollary 4.9]
, there is a natural fully faithful left exact left adjoint

\Sh^{\post}(T)\hookrightarrow\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})_{/T}

from the Postnikov completion of the
\infty
-topos of sheaves on
T
to condensed anima sliced over
T
.
By
Lemmas

2.28
and
2.29
, we deduce that this algebraic morphism induces an equivalence on protruncated shapes

\Pi_{<\infty}(\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})_{/T})\similarrightarrow\Pi_{<\infty}(\Sh^{\post}(T))\simeq\Pi_{<\infty}(T)\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Note that for any
\infty
-topos
\mathcal{X}
and object
U\smallin\mathcal{X}
, the forgetful functor
\mathcal{X}_{/U}\to\mathcal{X}
is left adjoint to the pullback functor
U\times(-)\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}_{/U}
.
Hence the shape of
\mathcal{X}_{/U}
coincides with the image of
U
under
\Gamma_{\sharp}\colon\mathcal{X}\to\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}})
.
Thus by
2.26
, the protruncated shape of the slice
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})_{/T}
coincides with prodiscretization of the condensed set
T
.
∎

2.33 Remark
.

Lemma

2.32
was also (essentially) observed in
[
Aok24
, Theorem 4.12]
.

2.5
Recollection on proétale sheaves

We now turn to recalling some background about the proétale topology and proétale sheaves.
The following definition is from
[
BhattScholzeProetale
]
:

2.34 Definition
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a morphism of schemes.

(1)

We call
f\colon X\to Y

weakly étale
if both
f
and its diagonal
\Delta_{f}
are flat.

(2)

We write
\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}_{X}
for the
proétale site of
X
, i.e., the site of weakly étale
X
-schemes equipped with the fpqc topology.

(3)

We furthermore write
X_{\proet}\coloneq\Sh(\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}_{X})
for the
proétale
\infty
-topos of
X
.

2.35
.

We almost exclusively work with the
hypercomplete
proétale
\infty
-topos
X_{\proet}^{\hyp}
.

2.36 Remark

((size issues))
.

Since the category of weakly étale
X
-schemes is not small,
Definition

2.34
introduces some set-theoretic issues.
In the end, one can always circumvent these issues and they do not have any serious effect on our results.
For the more cautious reader, we suggest one of the following two ways of reading this paper:

(1)

Fix once and for all two strongly inaccessible cardinals
\delta<\varepsilon
.
All schemes, spectral spaces, etc. are then assumed to be
\delta
-small and all categorical constructions, such as taking sheaves on a site, are taken with respect to the larger universe determined by
\varepsilon
.
In particular
X_{\proet}^{\hyp}
always means hypersheaves of
\varepsilon
-small anima on
\delta
-small weakly étale
X
-schemes, and similarly for the
\infty
-category of condensed anima
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
.

(2)

If the reader does not want to work with universes, they may proceed as follows.
For a scheme
X
, choose a strong limit cardinal
\kappa
such that
X
is
\kappa
-small.
Write
\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}_{X,\kappa}
for the category of
\kappa
-small weakly étale
X
-schemes.
We then define

X_{\proet,\kappa}^{\hyp}\coloneq\Sh^{\hyp}(\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}_{X,\kappa})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

The assumption that
\kappa
is a strong limit cardinal guarantees that there are enough w-contractibles in
\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}_{X,\kappa}
, see
Definition

2.42
.
We then define

X_{\proet}^{\hyp}\coloneq\textstyle\colim_{\kappa}X_{\proet,\kappa}^{\hyp}

and similarly for the category of condensed anima.
This is also the approach taken by Clausen and Scholze
[
Sch19a
]
.

However, then some statements about
X_{\proet}^{\hyp}
and
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
, such as
Proposition

2.51
, are no longer true on the nose.
In such a case, to correct the result, one must make an implicit choice of strong limit cutoff cardinal
\kappa
, and
X_{\proet}^{\hyp}
should be understood as
X_{\proet,\kappa}^{\hyp}
.
In the end, a choice of such a
\kappa
is harmless and does not affect our results, see
Remark

3.18
.

The same discussion applies to the non-hypercomplete proétale
\infty
-topos
X_{\proet}
.

We now prove a generalization of
[
BhattScholzeProetale
, Lemma 5.1.2 & Corollary 5.1.6]
.

2.37 Notation
.

For a scheme
X
, we denote the inclusion
\mathrm{\acute{E}t}_{X}\to\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}_{X}
of the the étale site into the proétale site by
\nu
.

2.38 Proposition
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme.
Then the pullback functor
\nu^{{\mathbin{*}}}\colon X_{\et}^{\hyp}\to X_{\proet}^{\hyp}
is fully faithful when restricted to truncated objects.

2.39 Notation
.

Let
X
be a scheme.
Write
\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\kern 0.89256pt\textup{aff}}\subset\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}_{X}
for the full subcategory spanned by the affine schemes.
Note that
\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\kern 0.89256pt\textup{aff}}
is a basis for the proétale topology on
\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}_{X}
.
Hence by
[
arXiv:2001.00319
, Corollary A.7]
, restriction along the inclusion defines an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

X_{\proet}^{\hyp}=\Sh^{\hyp}(\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}_{X})\similarrightarrow\Sh^{\hyp}(\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\kern 0.89256pt\textup{aff}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Proof of
Proposition

2.38
.

First observe that since the left exact pullback functor
\nu^{{\mathbin{*}}}
preserves
n
-truncated objects
[
HTT
,
Proposition 5.5.6.16
]
, the truncated pullback functors are well-defined.
We equivalently need to show that the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\et}^{\hyp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\proet}^{\hyp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sh^{\hyp}(\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\kern 0.89256pt\textup{aff}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{{\mathbin{*}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

is fully faithful when restricted to truncated objects.
To simplify notation, we also denote this composite by
\nu^{{\mathbin{*}}}
.

First observe that a presheaf of
n
-truncated anima
F\colon(\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\kern 0.89256pt\textup{aff}})^{\op}\to\textbf{{Ani}}_{\leq n}
is a sheaf if and only if the following conditions hold:

(1)

The presheaf
F
sends finite disjoint unions of affine schemes proétale over
X
to finite products.

(2)

For every surjection
f\colon U\twoheadrightarrow X
of affine schemes proétale over
X
with associated Čech nerve
U_{\bullet}\to X
, the canonical map

F(X)\to\lim_{[i]\smallin\mathbf{\Delta}_{\leq n+1}}F(U_{i})

is an isomorphism.

This is just the
n
-truncation of the sheaf condition as formulated in
[
SAG
,
Proposition A.3.3.1
]
,
3
3

3

One easily checks that the category
\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\kern 0.81949pt\textup{aff}}\subset\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}_{X}
satisfies the conditions stated there.

using the fact that totalizations in an
(n+1)
-category can be calculated as limits over
\mathbf{\Delta}_{\leq n+1}

[
arXiv:2207.09256
, Proposition A.1]
.

Since the problem is local on
X
, we immediate reduce to the case where
X
is affine.
Then, the category
\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\kern 0.89256pt\textup{aff}}
is exactly given by those
U\smallin\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}_{X}
which can be written as a small cofiltered limit
U=\lim_{i\smallin I}U_{i}

of affine schemes
U_{i}\smallin\mathrm{\acute{E}t}_{X}
.
Now let
n\geq 0
be an integer and, let
F
be an object of
X_{\et,\leq n}
.
The presheaf pullback of
F
to the proétale site of
X
is given by the formula
U\mapsto\colim_{i\smallin I^{\op}}F(U_{i})
on all
U\smallin\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\kern 0.89256pt\textup{aff}}
.
We wish to show, that this is already a sheaf.
For this, we can just copy the proof of
[
Lur18
, Proposition 7.1.3(2)]
.
The argument there works not only for equalizers, but for all finite limits as they appear in our
n
-truncated sheaf condition.
As
\nu^{*}F
restricts to
F
on affine étale schemes
\mathrm{\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\textup{aff}}
, it is clear that we have
\nu_{{\mathbin{*}}}\nu^{{\mathbin{*}}}F=F
for all
F\smallin X_{\et,\leq n}
, i.e., the pullback
\nu^{{\mathbin{*}}}
is fully faithful when restricted to
n
-truncated objects.
See
[
Mai25
, Proposition A.5.33]
for more details.
∎

Now we deduce some consequences for the étale homotopy type.
For this, recall our notation regarding shape theory from
2.25
.

2.40 Notation
.

Let
X
be a scheme.
We write

\Pi_{<\infty}^{\et}(X)\coloneq\Pi_{<\infty}(X_{\et}^{\hyp})\text{\hskip 23.49976ptand\hskip 23.49976pt}\widehat{\Pi}_{\infty}^{\et}(X)\coloneq\widehat{\Pi}_{\infty}(X_{\et}^{\hyp})

for the
protruncated étale homotopy type
and the
profinite étale homotopy type
of
X
, respectively.

2.41 Corollary
.

Let
X
be a scheme.
Then the map

\Pi_{<\infty}(\nu_{{\mathbin{*}}})\colon\Pi_{<\infty}(X_{\proet}^{\hyp})\to\Pi_{<\infty}^{\et}(X)

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Immediate from
Lemmas

2.28
and
2.38
.
∎

Basis of weakly contractible objects

Recall that an object
Y
of a site
\mathcal{C}
is
weakly contractible
if every covering
U\twoheadrightarrow Y
admits a section.
In the proétale site, weakly contractible qcqs objects are given by
w-contractible
schemes.

2.42 Definition
.

A qcqs scheme
X
is
w-contractible
if every weakly étale surjection
U\twoheadrightarrow X
admits a section.

For the subsequent characterization of w-contractibles, recall the following fact on connected components of qcqs schemes.

2.43 Lemma

(
[
STK
,
Tag 0900
]
)
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme.
Then the set
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)
of connected components of
|X|
, endowed with the quotient topology induced by
|X|
, is a profinite set.

2.44 Definition
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme.
We say that
X
is
w-local
if the subspace
X_{\cl}\subset|X|
of closed points is closed and every connected component of
X
has a unique closed point.
We say that
X
is
w-strictly local
if
X
is
w-local
and every étale surjection
U\twoheadrightarrow X
admits a section.

2.45 Remark
.

As observed in
[
MR0289501
, Proposition 3.1]
, since a w-strictly local scheme is a retract of an affine scheme, every w-strictly local scheme is affine.

2.46 Remark
.

By
[
BhattScholzeProetale
, Lemma 2.2.9]
, a qcqs scheme
X
is w-strictly local if
X
is w-local and the local rings at all closed points are strictly henselian.

2.47 Example
.

Let
\bar{k}
be a separably closed field.
Then any qcqs weakly étale
\bar{k}
-scheme
X
is w-strictly local.
Indeed, such a scheme is zero dimensional and thus, by Serre’s cohomological characterization of affineness, affine.
By
[
STK
,
Tag 092Q
]
, it is therefore a cofiltered limit of finite disjoint unions of
\mathrm{Spec}(\bar{k})
and hence w-strictly local.

2.48 Recollection

(
[
STK
,
Tag 0982
]
)
.

A scheme
X
is
w-contractible
if and only if it is w-strictly local and
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)\smallin\ProFin
is extremally disconnected.
In particular, w-contractible schemes are affine.

2.49 Notation
.

For a scheme
X
, we write
\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\wc}\subset\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}_{X}
for the full subcategory spanned by the w-contractible schemes.

2.50 Recollection

(
[
STK
,
Tag 0990
]
)
.

The subcategory
\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\wc}\subset\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}_{X}
is a basis for the proétale topology.
But beware that
\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\wc}
is not closed under fiber products in
\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}_{X}
.

2.51 Proposition
.

Let
X
be a scheme.
Restriction along the inclusion of sites
\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\wc}\subset\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}_{X}
defines an equivalence of hypercomplete
\infty
-topoi

X_{\proet}^{\hyp}=\Sh^{\hyp}(\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}_{X})\similarrightarrow\Sh^{\hyp}(\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\wc})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Moreover, this
\infty
-topos can be identified with the
\infty
-topos of finite product-preserving presheaves

\mathrm{Fun}^{\times}(({\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\wc}})^{\op},\textbf{{Ani}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Proof.

This follows from
2.50
and
[
arXiv:2001.00319
, Corollary A.7]
combined with the defining property of w-contractible schemes.
Details are given in
[
Mai25
, Proposition 2.2.12]
.
∎

Part I
The condensed homotopy type

3
Three perspectives on the condensed homotopy type

In this section, we introduce the condensed homotopy type of a scheme
X
.
As explained in the introduction, we give three definitions, and prove that they are equivalent.
The first, given in
Section

3.1
, is the relative shape of the hypercomplete proétale
\infty
-topos
X_{\proet}^{\hyp}
over the
\infty
-topos
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
of condensed anima.
The second, given in
Section

3.2
, is as the unique hypercomplete proétale cosheaf whose value on a w-contractible affine
U
is the profinite set
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(U)
of connected components of
U
.
The last, given in
Section

3.3
, is as the condensed classifying anima of the Galois category
\mathrm{Gal}(X)
introduced by Barwick–Glasman–Haine
[
BGH20
]
.
In
Section

3.4
, we conclude the section with a sample computation: given a henselian local ring
R
with residue field
\kappa
, we show inclusion of the closed point induces an equivalence

{\BGal_{\kappa}\simeq\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathrm{Spec}(\kappa))}\similarrightarrow{\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathrm{Spec}(R))}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

3.1
Definition via the relative shape

For an
\infty
-topos
\mathcal{X}
, the idea of shape theory relies on the existence of a canonical colimit preserving functor
\Gamma_{\sharp}\colon\mathcal{X}\to\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}})
.
We define the condensed homotopy type of a qcqs scheme in the tradition of shape theory but relative to the base
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
.
To do this, we use the identification

X_{\proet}^{\hyp}\simeq\mathrm{Fun}^{\times}\left(({\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\wc}})^{\op},\textbf{{Ani}}\right)

of the hypercomplete proétale
\infty
-topos as the
\infty
-topos of finite-product preserving presheaves on the site of w-contractible weakly étale
X
-schemes (
Proposition

2.51
).

3.1 Definition
.

Let
X
be a scheme.
Write

\pi_{\sharp}\colon{\PSh(\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\wc})}\to{\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}

for the colimit-preserving extension of

\mathrm{\pi}_{0}\colon\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\wc}\to\textbf{{Extr}}\hookrightarrow\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})

along the Yoneda embedding.

3.2 Observation
.

The functor
\pi_{\sharp}
admits a right adjoint

\displaystyle\pi^{{\mathbin{*}}}\colon\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})

\displaystyle\to\PSh(\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\wc})

given by the assignment

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\mapsto[W\mapsto A(\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(W))]\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Note that since the functor
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}\colon{\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\wc}}\to{\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}
preserves finite disjoint unions, the right adjoint to
\pi_{\sharp}
factors through

\mathrm{Fun}^{\times}\left(({\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\wc}})^{\op},\textbf{{Ani}}\right)\subset\PSh(\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\wc})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

3.3 Notation
.

Given a scheme
X
, we also write
\pi_{\sharp}
for the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\proet}^{\hyp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}^{\times}\left(({\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\wc}})^{\op},\textbf{{Ani}}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\sharp}}

where the left-hand functor is the equivalence of
\infty
-topoi from
Proposition

2.51
.

Next, we need a generalization of
[
BhattScholzeProetale
, Lemma 4.2.13]
.

3.4 Proposition
.

Let
X
be a scheme.
Then:

(1)

The functor
\pi_{\sharp}\colon{X_{\proet}^{\hyp}}\to{\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}
is left adjoint to
\pi^{{\mathbin{*}}}\colon{\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}\to{X_{\proet}^{\hyp}}
.

(2)

For each condensed anima
A
and w-contractible affine
W\smallin\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}_{X}
, there is a natural equivalence

\pi^{{\mathbin{*}}}(A)(W)\simeq A(\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(W))\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Proof.

As explained in
3.2
, the functor

\pi^{{\mathbin{*}}}\colon\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})\to\PSh(\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\wc})

factors through
X_{\proet}^{\hyp}
.
Hence
\pi^{{\mathbin{*}}}
remains right adjoint to the restriction of
\pi_{\sharp}
.
In particular, we have
\pi^{{\mathbin{*}}}(A)(U)\simeq A(\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(U))
.
∎

3.5 Remark
.

The right adjoint
\pi^{{\mathbin{*}}}
is part of a geometric morphism of
\infty
-topoi

(3.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\proet}^{\hyp}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{{\mathbin{*}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{{\mathbin{*}}}}

which is induced by the morphism of sites

\displaystyle\pi\colon\Pro(\textbf{{Set}}_{\textup{fin}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathrm{Pro\acute{E}t}_{X}

\displaystyle S=\lim_{i\smallin I}S_{i}

\displaystyle\longmapsto S\otimes X\coloneq\lim_{i\smallin I}\coprod_{s\smallin S_{i}}X\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

For details, see
[
Mai25
, Theorem 2.2.13]
.

Now we are ready for the definition of the condensed homotopy type.

3.7 Definition
.

Let
X
be a scheme.

(1)

The
condensed homotopy type
of
X
is the condensed anima

\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)\coloneq\pi_{\sharp}(1)\smallin\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

(2)

The
condensed set of connected components
of
X
is the condensed set

\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)\coloneq\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X))\smallin\Cond(\textbf{{Set}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

3.8
.

The first part of
Definition

3.7
says that the condensed homotopy type is the relative shape of the
\infty
-topos
X_{\proet}^{\hyp}
over the
\infty
-topos
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
, see
[
CE18
, §4.1]
for background on relative shapes.
Since sending a scheme
X
to
\pi_{{\mathbin{*}}}\colon X_{\proet}^{\hyp}\to\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
defines a functor

\textbf{{Sch}}\to(\textbf{{RTop}}_{\infty})_{/\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}

composition with the relative shape over
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
, therefore defines a functor

(3.9)

\displaystyle\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}\colon\textbf{{Sch}}\to\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}\hskip 11.74988ptX\mapsto\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

3.10 Warning
.

A consequence of the statement of
[
BhattScholzeProetale
, Lemma 4.2.13]
, is that that for any condensed set
A
, the formula
\pi^{{\mathbin{*}}}(A)(U)\simeq A(\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(U))
in
Proposition

3.4
holds for all qcqs schemes
U
of the proétale site of
X
.
However, this is not correct; indeed, if this stronger claim were true, it would follow that for all qcqs schemes
X
one has

\displaystyle\mathrm{Map}_{\Cond(\textbf{{Set}})}(\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X),A)

\displaystyle\simeq A(\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X))\simeq\pi^{*}(A)(X)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{X_{\proet}^{\hyp}}(X,\pi^{*}(A))

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}(\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X),A)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\Cond(\textbf{{Set}})}(\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X),A)\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

This would then imply that the condensed set of connected components matches the usual one, i.e.,
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)=\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)
in
\Cond(\textbf{{Set}})
.
As we show in
Example

4.26
, this is not generally the case.
However, this is true if
X
has finitely many irreducible components, see
Corollary

4.19
.
The problem here is that the proof of
[
BhattScholzeProetale
, Lemma 4.2.13]
only works for w-contractible schemes.

The definition tells us the value of the condensed homotopy type on w-contractible schemes:

3.11 Example
.

Let
W
be a w-contractible scheme.
Then, by definition,

\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(W)=\pi_{\sharp}(1)=\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(W)\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

In particular, if
W
is the spectrum of a separably closed field, then
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(W)={\mathbin{*}}
.

3.12
.

One consequence of
Example

3.11
is that every geometric point
\bar{x}\to X
defines a point

{\mathbin{*}}=\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\bar{x})\to\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)

of the condensed homotopy type.
Thus we can take homotopy groups at geometric points:

3.13 Definition
.

Let
X
be a scheme, let
\bar{x}\to X
be a geometric point, and let
n\geq 1
.
The
n
-th condensed homotopy group
of
X
at
\bar{x}
is the condensed group (abelian if
n\geq 2
)

\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{n}(X,\bar{x})\coloneq\mathrm{\pi}_{n}(\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X),\bar{x})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

From the definition, it is easy to see that the condensed homotopy type refines the protruncated and profinite étale homotopy types.
For this result, recall our notation on shapes and étale homotopy types from
Sections

2.4
and
2.40
.

3.14 Lemma
.

Let
X
be a scheme.
Then there are natural equivalences

\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)^{\wedge}_{\disc}\simeq\Pi_{<\infty}^{\et}(X)\text{\hskip 24.00024ptand\hskip 24.00024pt}\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)_{\mathrm{\pi}}^{\wedge}\simeq\widehat{\Pi}_{\infty}^{\et}(X)\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Proof.

By
Corollary

2.41
, the protruncated shapes of the (hypercomplete) étale and proétale
\infty
-topoi agree.
This remains true after profinite completion.
Thus the claims follow from the claim that the triangle of left adjoints

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\proet}^{\hyp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{<\infty}^{\et}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\wedge}_{\disc}}

commutes.
To see this, note that the corresponding diagram of right adjoints commutes by the uniqueness property of the pro-extension
\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}})\to X_{\proet}^{\hyp}
of the constant sheaf functor.
∎

3.15
.

The unit of the adjunction
(-)^{\wedge}_{\disc}\colon\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})\rightleftarrows\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty})
induces canonical comparison maps

\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)\to\Pi_{<\infty}^{\et}(X)\text{\hskip 23.49976ptand\hskip 23.49976pt}\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)\to\widehat{\Pi}_{\infty}^{\et}(X)

in
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
.
In particular, there are canonical comparison homomorphisms

\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{n}(X)\to\mathrm{\pi}^{\et}_{n}(X)

of the condensed homotopy groups to the (profinite) étale homotopy groups for all
n\geq 0
.

3.2
Characterization as a hypercomplete proétale cosheaf

The goal of this subsection is to prove the following characterization of the condensed homotopy type and derive some consequences for the étale homotopy type.

3.16 Notation
.

We write
\textbf{{Aff}}{}^{\kern 1.3387pt\wc}\subset\textbf{{Sch}}
for the full subcategory spanned by the w-contractible schemes.
(Recall from
2.48
that w-contractible schemes are affine.)

3.17 Proposition
.

The condensed homotopy type

\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}\colon{\textbf{{Sch}}}\to{\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}

is the unique hypercomplete proétale cosheaf whose restriction to w-contractible schemes is given by the functor

\mathrm{\pi}_{0}\colon\textbf{{Aff}}{}^{\kern 1.3387pt\wc}\to\textbf{{Extr}}\subset\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Proof.

First notice that since
\pi_{\sharp}
preserves colimits, by definition
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}
carries proétale hypercoverings to colimit diagrams.
Moreover, by construction
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}
agrees with
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}
when restricted to w-contractible schemes (see
Example

3.11
).
Thus it suffices to show that every scheme admits a proétale hypercover by w-contractible schemes.
Since every scheme admits a Zariski cover by qcqs schemes, we can reduce to the qcqs case.
In this case, the claim is the content of
[
STK
,
Tag 09A1
]
.
∎

3.18 Remark

((on set theory))
.

Let
X
be a scheme and
\kappa
a strong limit cardinal such that
X
is
\kappa
-small.
Then there exists a hypercover by w-contractibles
W_{\bullet}\to X
such that
W_{n}
is
\kappa
-small for all
n
.
Hence the formula

\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)\simeq\textstyle\colim_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(W_{\bullet})

shows that for
\kappa<\kappa^{\prime}
an implicit choice of cutoff cardinal in
Definition

3.7
does not affect the outcome.
More precisely, under the embedding
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})_{\kappa}\hookrightarrow\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})_{\kappa^{\prime}}
one gets carried to the other.
Equivalently, if one takes the approach to dealing with set theory explained in
Remark

2.36
(
2
), then for all choices of suitable cutoff cardinals the images of the condensed homotopy type in the colimit
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})=\colim_{\kappa}\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})_{\kappa}
agree.
Therefore we can continue to leave choices of cutoff cardinals implicit without getting into trouble.

If one would try to set up the theory in the setting of
light
condensed anima, one would get a different result in general.
See also
Remark

3.44
.

3.19 Corollary
.

(1)

The protruncated étale homotopy type
\Pi_{<\infty}^{\et}\colon{\textbf{{Sch}}}\to{\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty})}
is the unique hypercomplete proétale cosheaf valued in
\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty})
whose restriction to w-contractible affines coincides with

\mathrm{\pi}_{0}\colon\textbf{{Aff}}{}^{\kern 1.3387pt\wc}\to\textbf{{Extr}}\hookrightarrow\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

(2)

The profinite étale homotopy type
\widehat{\Pi}_{\infty}^{\et}\colon{\textbf{{Sch}}}\to{\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}})}
is the unique hypercomplete proétale cosheaf valued in
\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}})
whose restriction to w-contractible affines coincides with

\mathrm{\pi}_{0}\colon\textbf{{Aff}}{}^{\kern 1.3387pt\wc}\to\textbf{{Extr}}\hookrightarrow\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Proof.

Since both
(-)^{\wedge}_{\disc}
and
(-)_{\mathrm{\pi}}^{\wedge}
are left adjoints, the composites

Sch
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\wedge}_{\disc}}

and

Sch
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{\mathrm{\pi}}^{\wedge}}

are still hypercomplete proétale cosheaves.
Moreover, on w-contractible affines they both are given by
{U}\mapsto{\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(U)}\smallin\textbf{{Extr}}
.
In
Lemma

3.14
, we have seen that these functors recover the protruncated and profinite étale homotopy types, respectively.
∎

3.20 Remark
.

It follows immediately from
Proposition

3.17
that the ‘condensed shape’ defined in
[
hemo2023constructible_sheaves_schemes
, Appendix A]
agrees with our notions.

In
[
hemo2023constructible_sheaves_schemes
]
, Hemo–Richarz–Scholbach prove that
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)
classifies local systems on
X
with coefficients in any condensed ring.
We recall the precise statement here; for this, we need the following definition from
[
hemo2023constructible_sheaves_schemes
]
.
In order to state it, recall that we write
\pi^{{\mathbin{*}}}
for the natural pullback functor
{\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}\to{X_{\proet}^{\hyp}}
of
3.2
.

3.21 Definition
.

Let
\Lambda
be a condensed ring.

(1)

We define the condensed
\infty
-category
\textbf{{Perf}}_{\Lambda}
of
perfect complexes
over
\Lambda
, to be the condensed
\infty
-category defined by

\displaystyle\textbf{{Extr}}^{\op}\to\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}\hskip 23.49976ptS\mapsto\Perf_{\Lambda(S)}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Here,
\Perf_{\Lambda(S)}
is the usual
\infty
-category of perfect complexes over the ordinary ring
\Lambda(S)
.

(2)

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme.
Write
\mathrm{D}(X_{\proet};\Lambda)
for the derived
\infty
-category of
\pi^{*}\Lambda
-modules on
X
.
We define the
\infty
-category of
lisse

\Lambda
-modules
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{lis}}(X_{\proet};\Lambda)
to be the full subcategory of
\mathrm{D}(X_{\proet};\Lambda)
spanned by the dualizable objects.
