3.22 Proposition

(
[
hemo2023constructible_sheaves_schemes
, Proposition A.1]
)
.

There is a natural equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X),\textbf{{Perf}}_{\Lambda})\simeq\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{lis}}(X_{\proet};\Lambda)\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

3.23 Remark
.

Proposition

3.22
is one of the main motivations to study the condensed homotopy type.
Indeed, the analogous statement for the ususal étale homotopy type
\Pi_{\infty}^{\et}(X)
is not even true in for
\Lambda=\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}
.
See
[
BS15
, Example 7.4.9]
for a concrete counterexample.

3.3
Definition via exodromy

In this subsection, we explain why the
pyknotic étale homotopy type
defined in
[
BGH20
, Remark 13.8.10]
agrees with
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)
.
For this, we recall the following definition from
[
BGH20
]
in the general setting of coherent
\infty
-topoi, but we are most interested in the case of the étale
\infty
-topos of a scheme.
In order to understand the general definition, the reader may wish to review the theory of coherent
\infty
-topoi from
[
SAG
,
Appendix A
]
or
[
BGH20
, Chapter 3]
.

3.24 Definition
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a coherent
\infty
-topos.
The
Galois
\infty
-category
of
\mathcal{X}
is the condensed
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Gal}(\mathcal{X})
defined by the functor

\displaystyle\Pro(\textbf{{Set}}_{\textup{fin}})^{\op}

\displaystyle\to\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty}

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathrm{Fun}^{*,\coh}(\mathcal{X},\Sh(S))\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Here,
\mathrm{Fun}^{*,\coh}(\mathcal{X},\Sh(S))
is the
\infty
-category of
coherent
algebraic morphisms
s^{{\mathbin{*}}}\colon\mathcal{X}\to\Sh(S)
of
\infty
-topoi, i.e., those left exact left adjoints that send truncated coherent objects of
\mathcal{X}
to locally constant constructible sheaves of anima on the topological space
S
.

The assignment
{\mathcal{X}}\mapsto{\mathrm{Gal}(\mathcal{X})}
defines a functor from the
\infty
-category of coherent
\infty
-topoi and coherent geometric morphisms to
\Cond(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})
.

Now we explain what this definition means more concretely in the two examples we are interested in.

3.25 Recollection
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme.
Then the
\infty
-topos
X_{\et}
is coherent and by
[
BGH20
, Lemma 9.5.3 & Proposition 9.5.4]
, the truncated coherent objects of
X_{\et}
are the constructible étale sheaves of anima on
X
.

3.26 Notation
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme.
We write
\mathrm{Gal}(X)\coloneq\mathrm{Gal}(X_{\et})
.

3.27 Recollection
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme.
Since the
\infty
-topos
X_{\et}
is
1
-localic, for a profinite set
S
, the value
\mathrm{Gal}(X)(S)
is equivalent to the
1
-category of algebraic morphisms of
1
-topoi

s^{{\mathbin{*}}}\colon X_{\et,\leq 0}\to\Sh(S)_{\leq 0}

that send constructible étale sheaves of sets to locally constant constructible sheaves of sets on
S
.
In particular, the global sections
\mathrm{Gal}(X)({\mathbin{*}})
recovers the category of points
\mathrm{Pt}(X_{\et})
of the étale topos of
X
.

3.28 Recollection
.

Let
T
be a spectral space (e.g., the underlying space of a qcqs scheme).
Then the
\infty
-topos
\Sh(T)
is coherent and by
[
BGH20
, Lemma 9.5.3 & Proposition 9.5.4]
, the truncated coherent objects of
\Sh(T)
are the constructible sheaves of anima on
T
.

3.29 Notation
.

For a spectral space
T
, we write
\mathrm{Gal}(T_{\zar})\coloneq\mathrm{Gal}(\Sh(T))
.

3.30 Recollection
.

Let
T
be a spectral space.
Since spectral spaces are sober, by
[
BGH20
, Example 3.7.1]
and
[
HTT
,
Remark 6.4.5.3
]
, for a profinite set
S
, the value
\mathrm{Gal}(T_{\zar})(S)
is equivalent to the
poset
of quasicompact maps
f\colon S\to T
ordered by
pointwise specialization:

f\leq g
if and only if for all
s\smallin S
, we have
f(s)\smallin\overline{\{g(s)\}}
.
In particular,
\mathrm{Gal}(T_{\zar})({\mathbin{*}})
recovers the specialization poset of
T
.

3.31 Remark
.

Note that the condensed set underlying the condensed poset
\mathrm{Gal}(T_{\zar})
is indeed a condensed set, i.e., is
\kappa
-accessible for some
\kappa
.
In contrast, the condensed set represented by the topological space
T
is typically not
\kappa
-accessible, see
[
Sch19a
, Warning 2.14]
.
The difference between the two is that
\mathrm{Gal}(T_{\zar})(S)
is given by the set of
quasicompact
maps
S\to T
, as opposed to all continuous maps.

3.32 Recollection
.

For a qcqs scheme
X
, the condensed
\infty
-categories
\mathrm{Gal}(X)
and
\mathrm{Gal}(X_{\zar})
are in the image of the fully faithful functor

\iota\colon\mathrm{Cat}(\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}}))\to\Cond(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})

of
2.21
.
In fact, if we denote by
\textbf{{Lay}}_{\mathrm{\pi}}
the full subcategory of
\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty}
spanned by
\mathrm{\pi}
-finite layered categories in the sense of
[
BGH20
, Definition 2.3.7]
, then
\mathrm{Gal}(X)
and
\mathrm{Gal}(X_{\zar})
are even in the image of the fully faithful functor
\Pro(\textbf{{Lay}}_{\mathrm{\pi}})\to\Cond(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})
.
See
[
BGH20
, §13.5]
for more details.

Now we fix some notation regarding condensed
\infty
-categories and classifying anima.

3.33 Definition
.

We define condensed
\infty
-categories
\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Ani}})
and
\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Set}})
by the assignments

S\mapsto\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})_{/S}\text{\hskip 23.49976ptand\hskip 23.49976pt}S\mapsto\Cond(\textbf{{Set}})_{/S}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}

respectively.

3.34 Notation
.

We denote the left adjoint to the inclusion
\textbf{{Ani}}\hookrightarrow\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty}
by
\mathrm{B}\colon{\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty}}\to{\textbf{{Ani}}}
.
Given an
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
, we call
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{C}
the
classifying anima
of
\mathcal{C}
.

3.35
.

The functor
\mathrm{B}
preserves finite products.
Hence post-composition with
\mathrm{B}
induces a functor

\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}\colon{\Cond(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})}\to{\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}

that is left adjoint to the inclusion
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})\hookrightarrow\Cond(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})
.

3.36 Definition
.

Given a condensed
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
, we call
\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathcal{C})\smallin\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
the
condensed classifying anima
of
\mathcal{C}
.

To see the desired comparison, the idea is that, by
[
Wol22
, Corollary 1.2]
, we have a natural equivalence

\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{Gal}(X),\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Ani}}))\simeq X_{\proet}^{\hyp}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

In other words, in the condensed world,
X_{\proet}^{\hyp}
is a presheaf
\infty
-category on
\mathrm{Gal}(X)^{\op}
.
But the shape of a presheaf
\infty
-topos is given by taking the classifying anima of the
\infty
-category that it is presheaves on; the same holds in the condensed world.

3.37 Remark
.

An independent and more direct proof of
[
Wol22
, Corollary 1.2]
is going to appear in
[
vDW25
]
.

3.38 Proposition
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme.
Then there is a natural equivalence of condensed anima

\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)\simeq\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}\mathrm{Gal}(X)\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Proof.

This follows immediately from combining
[
Wol22
, Theorem 1.2]
and
[
MW24
, Proposition 4.4.1]
.
For the reader not so familiar with the theory developed in
[
MW24
]
, we spell out a more hands-on proof.
Recall that for
\infty
-categories
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
, the functor

\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{B}\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})

induced by precomposition along
\mathcal{C}\to\mathrm{B}\mathcal{C}
is fully faithful (since
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{C}\simeq\mathcal{C}[\mathcal{C}^{-1}]
is the localization of
\mathcal{C}
obtained by inverting all maps, this follows from the universal property of localization).
Since limits of fully faithful functors are fully faithful
[
HRS25
,
Mai25
]
, it follows that precomposition with
b\colon\mathrm{Gal}(X)\to\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}\mathrm{Gal}(X)
defines a fully faithful functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}\mathrm{Gal}(X),\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Ani}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{Gal}(X),\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Ani}}))\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b^{*}}

Furthermore, by
[
Wol22
, Lemma 4.3]
this functor admits a left adjoint
b_{\sharp}
.

By
[
Wol22
, Corollary 1.2]
we have a natural equivalence
X_{\proet}^{\hyp}\simeq\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{Gal}(X),\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Ani}}))
.
Under this equivalence the functor

\pi^{*}\colon\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})\to X_{\proet}^{\hyp}

agrees with the functor given by precomposing with the unique morphism
\mathrm{Gal}(X)\to{\mathbin{*}}.

We write
a\colon\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}\mathrm{Gal}(X)\to{\mathbin{*}}
for the unique morphism, and obtain a commutative triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}\mathrm{Gal}(X),\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Ani}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\proet}^{\hyp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}

.
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b^{{\mathbin{*}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{{\mathbin{*}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{{\mathbin{*}}}}

But now since
b^{*}
is fully faithful and
b^{*}(1)=1
, it follows that
b_{\sharp}(1)=1
,
Thus,

\pi_{\sharp}(1)=a_{\sharp}b_{\sharp}(1)=a_{\sharp}(1)\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Finally, by
[
Wol22
, Corollary 3.20]
we have

\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}\mathrm{Gal}(X),\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Ani}}))\simeq\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})_{/\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}\mathrm{Gal}(X)}

and the functor
a_{\sharp}
identifies with the forgetful functor.
In particular
a_{\sharp}(1)\simeq\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}\mathrm{Gal}(X)
.
∎

3.39 Remark
.

In particular,
Proposition

3.38
shows that if
X
is a qcqs scheme with finitely many irreducible components, then the underlying group
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{1}(X,\bar{x})({\mathbin{*}})
coincides with Gabber’s version of the proétale fundamental group, see
[
BS15
, Remark 7.4.12]
.

3.40 Corollary
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme.
If
\dim(X)=0
, then
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)=\mathrm{Gal}(X)
and this condensed anima is a
1
-truncated profinite anima.

Proof.

This is immediate from
[
HHW24a
, Observation 1.25]
and
3.32
.
∎

3.41 Example

((
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}
of a field))
.

Let
k
be a field and choose a separable closure
\bar{k}
of
k
.
Write
\mathrm{Gal}_{k}
for the absolute Galois group of
k
with respect to
\bar{k}
.
Then the choice of separable closure induces an equivalence

\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathrm{Spec}(k))=\mathrm{Gal}(\mathrm{Spec}(k))\simeq\BGal_{k}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

The left-hand identification follows from
Corollary

3.40
, and the right-hand identification follows from
[
BGH20
, Examples 11.2.1 and 12.2.1]
.

We do not use the next corollary in the remainder of this article, but we include it for completeness:

3.42 Corollary
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme.
If
\dim(X)=0
, then
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)={\mathbin{*}}
if and only if the reduced scheme
X_{\red}
is
\mathrm{Spec}(k)
for
k
a separably closed field.

Proof.

As the étale
\infty
-topos is invariant under universal homeomorphisms, the same holds for
\mathrm{Gal}
and therefore
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}
.
As
X\to X_{\red}
is a universal homeomorphism, the if direction follows by the
Example

3.41
.
For the reverse direction, note that
\mathrm{Gal}(X)(*)=\Pt(X_{\et})
of a
0
-dimensional affine scheme is contractible only if
X=\mathrm{Spec}(R)
for
R
a local ring with separably closed residue field
k
.
For such a scheme, it is
X_{\red}=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
.
∎

3.4
Computation:
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}
of henselian local rings

We conclude this section by explaining how to use the definitions to show that the condensed homotopy type of a w-strictly local scheme
X
(in the sense of
Definition

2.44
) agrees with the profinite set
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)
of connected components of
X
.
This allows for a direct computation of the condensed homotopy type of a henselian local ring.

3.43 Proposition
.

Let
X
be a w-strictly local scheme.
Then
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)\simeq\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)
.

3.44 Remark
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme that locally can be written as the spectrum of a countable colimit of finite type
\mathbf{Z}
-algebras.
Then one can show that there is a hypercover
W_{\bullet}\to X
consisting of w-strictly local
X
-schemes with the property that
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)
is a light condensed set.
Hence it follows from
Proposition

3.43
that in this case
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)
is a light condensed anima in the sense that it is in the image of the fully faithful functor

\Sh(\ProFin_{\aleph_{1}})\hookrightarrow\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

For a general scheme
X
, the condensed homotopy type
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)
need not be light.

Recall that the proétale site is “tensored” over profinite sets (cf.
[
BhattScholzeProetale
, Example 4.1.9]
).

3.45 Recollection
.

Let
X
be an affine scheme and
f_{\!0}\colon S\to\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)
a map from a profinite set.
Recall that the pullback of topological spaces
|X|\times_{\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)}S
naturally has the structure of an affine scheme that we denote by
X\otimes_{\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)}S
.
This affine scheme comes equipped with a proétale map
f\colon X\otimes_{\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)}S\to X
satisfying
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(f)=f_{\!0}
.
Moreover, this construction is functorial in both
X
and
S
.
See
[
BhattScholzeProetale
, Lemma 2.2.8]
for details.

3.46 Lemma
.

Let
X
be an affine scheme and
f_{\!0}\colon S\to\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)
a map from a profinite set.
If
X
is w-strictly local, then so is
X\otimes_{\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)}S
.

Proof.

Write
X^{\prime}\coloneq X\otimes_{\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)}S
.
We can split the construction of
X^{\prime}
into two steps: first consider
X^{\prime\prime}=X\otimes S
coming from “tensoring” by
S
.
It satisfies
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X^{\prime\prime})=\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)\times S
.
Then realize
X^{\prime}
as a closed subscheme of
X^{\prime\prime}
that is moreover an intersection of clopen subschemes, by looking at
S\subset\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)\times S=\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X^{\prime\prime})
and writing
S
as an intersection of clopen subsets in this larger set.

Let us first check it for
X^{\prime\prime}
.
By definition and
[
BhattScholzeProetale
, Lemma 2.2.9]
, an affine scheme is w-strictly local if it is w-local and all of its connected components are spectra of strictly henselian rings.
Here, we are using the following observation: the connected components of a w-local affine scheme are spectra of local rings.
Indeed, they are affine (being closed subschemes of an affine scheme) and have a single closed point (by definition of w-locality).
Thus, Zariski localizations at closed points of a w-local affine scheme match the corresponding connected components.

One checks that both of these conditions are satisfied for
X^{\prime\prime}=X\otimes S
by checking the following facts:

(1)

We have
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X\otimes S)=\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)\times S
.

(2)

Every connected component of
X\otimes S
is isomorphic (as a scheme) to some connected component of
X
.

(3)

We have
(X\otimes S)_{\cl}\simeq X_{\cl}\otimes S
.

Note that if
S=\lim_{i\smallin I}S_{i}
for finite sets
S_{i}
, then
X\otimes S
is defined as an inverse limit of the form
\lim_{i\smallin I}X^{S_{i}}=\lim_{i\smallin I}(X\sqcup\cdots\sqcup X)
where the transition maps restricted to each copy of
X
appearing there are just identities onto another copy of
X
.
As a result, each of the above points is reasonably easy to check.

The second step of passing from
X^{\prime\prime}
to
X^{\prime}
by intersecting an inverse system of clopen subschemes follows similarly.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

3.43
.

By
Proposition

3.17
, this statement holds when
X
is w-contractible.
In general, pick a hypercover of the profinite set
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)
by extremally disconnected profinite sets.
By
[
BhattScholzeProetale
, Lemma 2.2.8]
,
2.48
, and
Lemma

3.46
, we obtain a proétale hypercover
X_{\bullet}\to X
by w-contractible affine schemes
4
4

4

Here we have used that the functor in
loc. cit.
commutes with limits and respects covers.

that recovers the original hypercover of
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)
after applying
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}
.
We compute

\displaystyle\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)

\displaystyle\simeq\colim_{[n]\smallin\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X_{n})

\displaystyle\simeq\colim_{[n]\smallin\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X_{n})\simeq\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}

as desired.
∎

We now move on to the promised applications.

3.47 Corollary
.

Let
S
be a profinite set and
X
a w-strictly local scheme. Then

\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X\otimes S)\simeq\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)\times S\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Proof.

This follows from
Proposition

3.43
and
Lemma

3.46
with
f_{0}=\pr_{1}\colon\pi_{0}(X)\times S\to\pi_{0}(X)
together with the equality
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X\otimes S)=\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)\times S
.
∎

3.48 Corollary
.

Let
R
be a henselian local ring with residue field
\kappa
.
Then the inclusion of the closed point
\mathrm{Spec}(\kappa)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Spec}(R)
induces an equivalence

\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathrm{Spec}(\kappa))\similarrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathrm{Spec}(R))

and both are equivalent to
\BGal_{\kappa}
.

Proof.

Write
X=\mathrm{Spec}(R)
and
x=\mathrm{Spec}(\kappa)
.
Fix a separable closure
\overline{\kappa}
of
\kappa
and let
R^{\sh}
be the corresponding strict henselization.
Writing
\overline{\kappa}
as an increasing union of finite separable extensions (and using that
\FEt_{x}\simeq\FEt_{X}
) provides a presentation of
X^{\prime}=\mathrm{Spec}(R^{\sh})
as a pro-(finite étale) cover of
X
, see
[
STK
,
Tag 0BSL
]
.
Let
X_{\bullet}
be the Čech nerve of this cover
X^{\prime}\to X
.
As the equivalence
\FEt_{x}\simeq\FEt_{X}
extends to the categories of pro-objects, we compute that
X_{\bullet}
writes as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}\otimes\mathrm{Gal}_{\kappa}\times\mathrm{Gal}_{\kappa}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}\otimes\mathrm{Gal}_{\kappa}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}

compatibly with the analogous presentation of the Čech nerve
x_{\bullet}
of
\bar{x}=\mathrm{Spec}(\overline{\kappa}))\to\mathrm{Spec}(\kappa)=x
.
Applying
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}
to the corresponding “ladder” diagram (coming from the map
x_{\bullet}\to X_{\bullet}
) and using that, for every
m\smallin\mathbf{N}
,

\mathrm{Gal}_{\kappa}^{m}\simeq\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\bar{x}\otimes\mathrm{Gal}_{\kappa}^{m})\to\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X^{\prime}\otimes\mathrm{Gal}_{\kappa}^{m})\simeq\mathrm{Gal}_{\kappa}^{m}

is an isomorphism (where we are using
Corollary

3.47
and the fact that both
\bar{x}
and
X^{\prime}
are connected w-contractible schemes), we conclude.
∎

4
Connected components of the condensed homotopy type

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme.
In this section, we give an explicit description of the condensed set of connected components
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)
of the condensed homotopy type
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)
.
To do so, we make use of the Galois category
\mathrm{Gal}(X_{\zar})
of the Zariski
\infty
-topos in the sense of
Definition

3.24
.
In
Section

4.1
, we show that the condensed connected components of
\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}\mathrm{Gal}(X_{\zar})
agree with
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)
.
In
Section

4.2
, we use this description to show that if
X
has finitely many irreducible components, then
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)
agrees with the profinite set
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)
of connected components (
Corollary

4.19
).
We also give examples of connected schemes whose
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)
is nontrivial and show that
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)
can be quite exotic in general.
Finally, in
Section

4.3
, we use our explicit description of
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)
to compute the condensed and étale homotopy types of the ring of continuous functions from a compact Hausdorff space to
\mathbf{C}
, see
Corollary

4.35
.

4.1
Prozariski sheaves

Recall that for a scheme
X
, we will write
X_{\zar}
for the
\infty
-topos of Zariski sheaves on
X
.
In this subsection, we study a pro-version of the Zariski
\infty
-topos.

4.1 Definition
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme.
Let us write
X_{\zar}^{\cons}\subset X
for the full subcategory of Zariski sheaves, that is spanned by the
constructible
sheaves on
X
, i.e., those sheaves that are locally constant with
\mathrm{\pi}
-finite stalks on a finite constructible stratification of
X
.
We give
\Pro(X_{\zar}^{\cons})
the
effective epimorphism topology
where covers are generated by finite jointly effectively epimorphic families of maps.
We call the
\infty
-topos

X^{\hyp}_{\prozar}\coloneq\Sh_{\textup{eff}}^{\hyp}(\Pro(X_{\zar}^{\cons}))

of hypersheaves for the effective epimorphism topology on
\Pro(X_{\zar}^{\cons})
, the hypercomplete
prozariski topos
of
X
.
Since pullbacks along qcqs morphisms of schemes preserve constructible sheaves,
X^{\hyp}_{\prozar}
is functorial in
X
.

4.2 Remark
.

This construction makes sense more generally for any
bounded coherent

\infty
-topos (in the sense of
[
SAG
,
Appendix A
]
) and was called
solidification
in
[
BH19
]
and
pyknotification
in
[
zbMATH07671238
]
.

4.3
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme.
The pullback functor
X_{\zar}\to X_{\et}
preserves constructible sheaves and thus defines a functor

X_{\zar}^{\cons}\to X_{\et}^{\cons}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Extending to pro-objects we obtain a morphism of sites
\rho^{{\mathbin{*}}}\colon\Pro(X_{\zar}^{\cons})\to\Pro(X_{\et}^{\cons})
and thus an algebraic morphism of
\infty
-topoi

X_{\prozar}^{\hyp}\to\Sh_{\textup{eff}}^{\hyp}(\Pro(X_{\et}^{\cons}))\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Finally,
[
Lur18
, Example 7.1.7]
provides an equivalence
X_{\proet}^{\hyp}\simeq\Sh_{\textup{eff}}^{\hyp}(\Pro(X_{\et}^{\cons}))
so that we obtain an algebraic morphism

\rho^{{\mathbin{*}}}\colon X_{\prozar}^{\hyp}\to X_{\proet}^{\hyp}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Recall that a map
Y\to X
is a
Zariski localization
if
Y
is isomorphic (over
X
) to a finite disjoint union of open subschemes of
X
.

4.4
.

Let
X
be affine scheme.
We write
\mathrm{Zar}{}_{X}^{\kern 0.89256pt\textup{aff}}\subset\textbf{{Sch}}_{/X}
for the full subcategory spanned by the affine Zariski localizations of
X
.
Since open immersions between qcqs schemes are of finite presentation it follows from
[
STK
,
Tag 01ZC
]
that the canonical functor

\Pro(\mathrm{Zar}{}_{X}^{\kern 0.89256pt\textup{aff}})\to\textbf{{Sch}}_{/X}

is fully faithful.
Thus we may equip
\Pro(\mathrm{Zar}{}_{X}^{\kern 0.89256pt\textup{aff}})
with the fpqc topology.
Since the sheaf represented by a Zariski localization is constructible, we obtain a morphism of sites

\mu\colon\Pro(\mathrm{Zar}{}_{X}^{\kern 0.89256pt\textup{aff}})\to\Pro(X^{\cons}_{\zar})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

4.5 Lemma
.

Let
X
be an affine scheme.
Then the algebraic morphism of
\infty
-topoi

\mu^{{\mathbin{*}}}\colon\Sh^{\hyp}_{\textup{fpqc}}(\Pro(\mathrm{Zar}{}_{X}^{\kern 0.89256pt\textup{aff}}))\to X_{\prozar}^{\hyp}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

The proof is exactly the same as in
[
Lur18
, Example 7.1.7]
.
∎

4.6 Remark
.

Let
X
be an affine scheme.
Then under the equivalence of
Lemma

4.5
, the functor
\rho^{{\mathbin{*}}}
is induced by the morphism of sites

\Pro(\mathrm{Zar}{}_{X}^{\kern 0.89256pt\textup{aff}})\to\Pro(\mathrm{\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\textup{aff}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}

that comes from the inclusion
\mathrm{Zar}{}_{X}^{\kern 0.89256pt\textup{aff}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\textup{aff}}
.
Here
\mathrm{\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\textup{aff}}
denotes the category of affine étale
X
-schemes.

4.7 Recollection
.

For a qcqs scheme
X
, we write
\mathrm{Gal}(X_{\zar})
for the Galois category of the Zariski
\infty
-topos in the sense of
Definition

3.24
.
Note that
X_{\zar}
is the
\infty
-topos of sheaves on the spectral topological space
|X|
.
Hence by
3.30
, for a profinite set
S
, the category of sections
\mathrm{Gal}(X_{\zar})(S)
is the poset of continuous quasicompact maps
f\colon S\to\lvert X\rvert
ordered by pointwise specialization:
f\leq g
if and only if for all
s\smallin S
, we have
f(s)\smallin\overline{\{g(s)\}}
.
In particular,
\mathrm{Gal}(X_{\zar})({\mathbin{*}})
is the
specialization poset
of
|X|
.
To simplify notation, we denote the specialization poset of
|X|
by
{X}_{\zar}^{\leq}
.

4.8 Lemma
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme.
Then there is a natural equivalence of
\infty
-topoi

X_{\prozar}^{\hyp}\similarrightarrow\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{Gal}(X_{\zar}),\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Ani}}))\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Proof.

Since
X_{\zar}
is a spectral
\infty
-topos in the sense of
[
BGH20
, Definition 9.2.1]
and the profinite stratified shape of
X_{\zar}
is given by
\mathrm{Gal}(X_{\zar})
, this follows from
[
zbMATH07671238
, Theorem 1.1]
.
∎

We are interested in
Lemma

4.8
because it allows us to compute
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}
of the relative shape of prozariski
\infty
-topos over
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
via the condensed classifying anima of
\mathrm{Gal}(X_{\zar})
.
The latter turns out to be a quotient of the condensed set underlying
\mathrm{Gal}(X_{\zar})
by an explicit equivalence relation.
Furthermore, the next proposition readily implies that this actually computes
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)
:

4.9 Proposition
.

The functor
\rho^{{\mathbin{*}}}\colon X_{\prozar,\leq 0}\to X_{\proet,\leq 0}
is fully faithful.

In order to prove
Proposition

4.9
, we make use of the following construction:

4.10 Construction
.

Let
X
be an affine scheme.
Since the inclusion
\mathrm{Zar}{}_{X}^{\kern 0.89256pt\textup{aff}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\textup{aff}}
preserves finite limits, it admits a pro-left adjoint

\HenszarX\colon\Pro(\mathrm{\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\textup{aff}})\to\Pro(\mathrm{Zar}{}_{X}^{\kern 0.89256pt\textup{aff}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

4.11 Definition

((Zariski henselization))
.

Let
X
be an affine scheme and
Y\smallin\Pro(\mathrm{\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\textup{aff}})
.
We call
\HenszarX(Y)
the
Zariski henselization of
Y
in
X
.

4.12 Lemma
.

Let
X
be an affine scheme and
V\smallin\Pro(\mathrm{\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\textup{aff}})
.
If
V
is w-contractible, the unit morphism
V\to\HenszarX(V)
is surjective.

Proof.

Since
V
is w-contractible, we can use the universal property of
\HenszarX(V)
to show that any pro-Zariski cover of
\HenszarX(V)
admits a section.
This in particular shows that
\HenszarX(V)
is w-local, see
[
BhattScholzeProetale
, Lemma 2.4.2]
.
Since
V\to\HenszarX(V)
is flat and the image of a flat morphism is closed under generization
[
AG1
, Lemma 14.9]
, it suffices to show that all closed points are in the image.

We now assume, for the sake of contradiction, that
\im(V)\subset\HenszarX(V)
does not contain a closed point
x
.
Since
\im(V)
is quasicompact, there is some quasicompact open
H\subset\HenszarX(V)
containing
\im(V)
such that
x\notin H
.
Since
H
is quasicompact, there exists a covering
(U_{i})_{i\smallin I}
of
H
by finitely many affine opens.
Since
\im(V)\subset H
, it follows that the induced map

\coprod_{i\smallin I}U_{i}\times_{\HenszarX(V)}V\to V

is surjective and thus admits a section
\alpha\colon V\to\coprod_{i\smallin I}U_{i}\times_{\HenszarX(V)}V
.
By the universal property of Zariski henselization, the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{i\smallin I}U_{i}\times_{\HenszarX(V)}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{i\smallin I}U_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

factors uniquely through some
\tilde{\alpha}\colon\HenszarX(V)\to\coprod_{i\smallin I}U_{i}
.
Since the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{i\smallin I}U_{i}\times_{\HenszarX(V)}V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{i\smallin I}U_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\HenszarX(V)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

recovers the unit
V\to\HenszarX(V)
, it follows by uniqueness that the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\HenszarX(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{i\smallin I}U_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\HenszarX(V)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\alpha}}

is the identity.
In particular the
U_{i}
cover
\HenszarX(V)
and thus
H=\HenszarX(V)
; this contradicts that
x\notin H
.
∎

4.13 Lemma
.

Let
X
be an affine scheme, and
F\smallin X_{\prozar}^{\hyp}
.
Then
\rho^{{\mathbin{*}}}(F)\smallin X_{\proet}^{\hyp}
is the hypersheafification of the presheaf

\Pro(\mathrm{\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\textup{aff}})^{\op}\to\textbf{{Ani}}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}\hskip 23.49976ptW\mapsto F(\HenszarX(W))\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Moreover, if
W
is w-contractible, then
\rho^{{\mathbin{*}}}(F)(W)=F(\HenszarX(W))
.

Proof.

The functor
\rho^{{\mathbin{*}}}
is given by the hypersheafification of the left Kan extension along the functor

\iota\colon\Pro(\mathrm{Zar}{}_{X}^{\kern 0.89256pt\textup{aff}})^{\op}\hookrightarrow\Pro(\mathrm{\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\textup{aff}})^{\op}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Explicitly, for
F\smallin X_{\prozar}^{\hyp}
the image is given by

(4.14)

\rho^{{\mathbin{*}}}(F)=\left(W\mapsto\colim_{W\to\iota(V)}F(V)\right)^{\dagger}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}

where
V\smallin\Pro(\mathrm{Zar}{}_{X}^{\kern 0.89256pt\textup{aff}})
,
W\smallin\Pro(\mathrm{\acute{E}t}{}_{X}^{\textup{aff}})
, and
(-)^{\dagger}
denotes hypersheafification.
By the universal property of Zariski henselization, every map
W\to\iota(V)
factors uniquely over
\HenszarX(W)
, hence the colimit in (
4.14
) reduces to

\colim_{W\to\iota(V)}F(V)=F(\HenszarX(W))\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

It remains to argue why hypersheafification does not change the value on a w-contractible scheme
W
.
On the basis of w-contractible schemes weakly étale over
X
, the sheaf condition simplifies to sending finite coproducts to finite products.
Moreover, every sheaf is a hypersheaf.
Since
\HenszarX
, being a left adjoint, preserves finite coproducts and
F
carries finite coproducts to finite products, the claim follows.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

4.9
.

We can immediately reduce to the case where
X
is affine.
We want to show that for any
F\smallin X_{\prozar,\leq 0}
and any
U\smallin\Pro(\mathrm{Zar}{}_{X}^{\kern 0.89256pt\textup{aff}})
the unit evaluated at
U

F(U)\to\rho^{{\mathbin{*}}}(F)(U)

is an isomorphism.
For this, pick a w-contractible weakly étale
X
-scheme
W
with a surjection
W\twoheadrightarrow U
and a further w-contractible
V
with a surjection
V\twoheadrightarrow W\times_{U}W
.
Using
Lemma

4.13
, it suffices to show that the natural map

F(U)\to\lim\left(F(\HenszarX(W))\rightrightarrows F(\HenszarX(V))\right)

is an isomorphism.
This is clear if we show that

\HenszarX(V)\rightrightarrows\HenszarX(W)\to U

is the beginning of an augmented pro-Zariski hypercover.

For this, first observe that since the surjection
W\twoheadrightarrow U
factors through the canonical map
\HenszarX(W)\to U
, the rightmost morphism above is surjective.
Note that we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\times_{U}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\HenszarX(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\HenszarX(W)\times_{U}\HenszarX(W)\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}}

Here, the top horizontal morphism is surjective by definition and the right vertical morphism is surjective by
Lemma

4.12
.
Thus the bottom horizontal morphism is also surjective, as desired.
∎

4.15 Warning
.

Proposition

4.9
is only true on the level of
0
-truncated sheaves, i.e., sheaves of sets.
Full faithfulness on the level of sheaves of anima would imply an equivalence of the condensed homotopy type with the relative shape of the the prozariski
\infty
-topos over
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
.
Therefore, it would also imply that the étale homotopy type of
X
agrees with the shape of the underlying topological space of
X
, which is generally false.

Note that if
X
is an everywhere strictly local scheme, by
[
Sch17
, Corollary 2.5]
one has
X_{\et}=X_{\zar}
.
So, in this case
\rho^{{\mathbin{*}}}
is fully faithful for all sheaves of anima.

4.2
An explicit description of
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}^{\mathrm{cond}}

In this subsection, we give an explicit description of
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)
.
To do this, we first observe that together the results from
Section

4.1
show:

4.16 Proposition
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme.
Then there is a natural isomorphism of condensed sets

\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)\similarrightarrow\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}\mathrm{Gal}(X_{\zar}))\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Proof.

Consider the morphism of sites
\tilde{\pi}\colon\ProFin\to\Pro(X_{\zar}^{\cons})
given by
S\mapsto S\times X
.
We have a commutative triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\prozar}^{\hyp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\proet}^{\hyp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\pi}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{{\mathbin{*}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{{\mathbin{*}}}}

Combining
Lemma

4.8
and
[
Wol22
, Lemma 4.3]
, it follows that
\tilde{\pi}^{*}
has a left adjoint, that we denote
\tilde{\pi}_{\sharp}
.
By
Proposition

4.9
, it follows that
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)\simeq\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(\tilde{\pi}_{\sharp}(1))
.
By
Lemma

4.8
, the same argument as in
Proposition

3.38
shows that
\tilde{\pi}_{\sharp}(1)\simeq\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}\mathrm{Gal}(X_{\zar})
.
Hence

\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)\simeq\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(\tilde{\pi}_{\sharp}(1))\simeq\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}\mathrm{Gal}(X_{\zar}))\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}

as desired.
∎

Proposition

4.16
lets us explicitly describe
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)
.

4.17 Remark
.

Let
S
be a profinite set and let
T
be a spectral space.
The next theorem involves sets of continuous quasicompact maps
\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S,T)
.
Note that these are those maps such that the preimage of a quasicompact open is clopen.
It follows that these are precisely continuous maps in the constuctible topology, i.e.,

\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S,T)=\mathrm{Map}(S,T^{\cons})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Said differently, the inclusion of the full subcategory of profinite sets into the category of spectral spaces and quasicompact maps admits a right adjoint, given by sending a spectral space
T
to the underlying set of
T
equipped with the constructible topology.

4.18 Theorem
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme.
Then for every extremally disconnected profinite set
S
, we have

\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)(S)\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S,\lvert X\rvert)/\kern-2.34993pt\sim\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}

where
f\sim g
if and only if there is some
n\smallin\mathbf{N}
and quasicompact maps
s_{1},t_{1},\ldots,s_{n},t_{n}\colon S\to\lvert X\rvert
such that

f\geq s_{1}\leq t_{1}\geq s_{2}\leq t_{2}\geq\cdots\geq s_{n}\leq t_{n}\geq g\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Here,
a\leq b
if and only if for all
s\smallin S
, we have
a(s)\smallin\overline{\{b(s)\}}
.

Moreover, if
S=\mathrm{\beta}(M)
, restriction along the canonical map
M\to\mathrm{\beta}(M)
induces an isomorphism

(\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S,\lvert X\rvert)/\kern-2.34993pt\sim)\similarrightarrow\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(({X}_{\zar}^{\leq})^{M})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Here,
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(({X}_{\zar}^{\leq})^{M})
is the quotient of
({X}_{\zar}^{\leq})^{M}
identifying two points
(x_{m})_{m\smallin M}
and
(y_{m})_{m\smallin M}
if and only if they can be connected by a finite zigzag of pointwise specializations.

Proof.

By
Proposition

4.16
, the first statement reduces to showing that for every extremally disconnected profinite set
S
, we have

\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}\mathrm{Gal}(X_{\zar}))(S)=\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S,\lvert X\rvert)/\kern-2.34993pt\sim\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

This follows by the description of
\mathrm{Gal}(X_{\zar})
in
4.7
noticing that maps
f,g
in the poset
\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S,\lvert X\rvert)
are connected if and only if there exists a finite zig-zag of pointwise specializations as indicated in the statement.

For the second statement, by
Proposition

2.22
, we have a chain of canonical equivalences of partially ordered sets

\displaystyle\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathrm{\beta}(M),\lvert X\rvert)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Gal}(X_{\zar})(\mathrm{\beta}(M))

\displaystyle\simeq\prod_{M}\mathrm{Gal}(X_{\zar})(*)=\prod_{M}{X}_{\zar}^{\leq}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}

where the second equivalence is induced by
M\to\mathrm{\beta}(M)
.
Under this identification, the equivalence relation generated by pointwise specialization corresponds to the equivalence relation defining
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(({X}_{\zar}^{\leq})^{M})
explained in the final statement.
This concludes the proof of the second claim.
∎

Theorem

4.18
shows that
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)
gives the expected answer in many cases of interest:

4.19 Corollary
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme with finitely many irreducible components.
Then the canonical map of condensed sets

\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)\to\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)

of
3.15
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

It suffices to check that the map is an isomorphism after evaluating at
\mathrm{\beta}(M)
for any discrete set
M
.
By
Theorem

4.18
, we need to see that the canonical map

\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(({X}_{\zar}^{\leq})^{M})\to\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)^{M}

that sends a function
M\to\lvert X\rvert
to the composite with
\lvert X\rvert\to\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)
is an isomorphism (note that this is not immediate, since in general
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}
does not commute with infinite products).
It is surjective by surjectivity of
\lvert X\rvert\to\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)
.
For injectivity, suppose that we have maps
f,g\colon M\to\lvert X\rvert
that agree after composing with
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}
.
If the number of irreducible components of
X
is
n
, it follows that we may connect any two points
x,y\smallin X
in the same connected component with a zig-zag of specializations involving at most
2n+1
other points.
Thus we may also connect
f
and
g
with a zig-zag involving
2n+1
other maps and thus
[f]=[g]
in
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(({X}_{\zar}^{\leq})^{M})
, as desired.
∎

4.20 Remark
.

For an alternative proof of
Corollary

4.19
, see
[
Mai25
, Proposition 2.2.25]
.

4.21 Observation
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme and let
\bar{x}\to X
and
\bar{x}^{\prime}\to X
be geometric points.
If
X
is connected and has finitely many irreducible components, then by
Corollary

4.19
,
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)={\mathbin{*}}
.
Hence, for each
n\geq 1
, there exists an isomorphism
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{n}(X,\bar{x})\simeq\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{n}(X,\bar{x}^{\prime})
.

In the remainder of this subsection, we provide some examples illustrating that
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)
can substantially differ from
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)
in general.
By
Proposition

4.16
,
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)
only depends on the spectral space
|X|
; so we formulate the following result only in terms of spectral spaces.

4.22 Recollection

(
[
FK18
, Chapter 0, §2.3]
)
.

A spectral space
T
is
valuative
if, for each
t\smallin T
, the set of generizations of
t
is totally ordered under the generization relation.
Every point
t
of a valuative space
T
has a unique maximal generization, denoted
t^{\max}
.

The
separated quotient
of a valuative spectral space
T
is the quotient
T^{\sep}\coloneq T/\kern-3.525pt\sim
by the relation
s\sim t
if
s^{\max}\sim t^{\max}
.
By
[
FK18
, Chapter 0, Corollary 2.3.18]
,
T^{\sep}
is a compact Hausdorff space.

For the next result, recall the Galois category of a spectral space from
3.29
and
3.30
.

4.23 Corollary
.

Let
T
be a valuative spectral space.
Then the natural map

\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(\mathrm{Gal}(T_{\zar}))\to T^{\sep}

is an isomorphism of condensed sets.

Proof.

It again suffices to check this after evaluating at the Čech–Stone
\mathrm{\beta}(M)
of any set
M
.
So let
\alpha\colon\mathrm{\beta}(M)\to T^{\sep}
be any continuous map.
Since the quotient map
\pi\colon T\to T^{\sep}
is surjective, we may pick a map
a\colon M\to T
lifting
\alpha|_{M}
.
Using
Proposition

2.22
as in
Theorem

4.18
,
a
extends to a quasicompact continuous map
\bar{a}\colon\mathrm{\beta}(M)\to T
and by construction we have
\pi\circ\bar{a}|_{M}=\alpha|_{M}
.
By the universal property of Čech–Stone compactification, we thus get
\pi\circ\bar{a}=\alpha
, proving surjectivity.
For injectivity, suppose that we are given maps
f,g\colon M\to T
such that the composites with
\pi
agree.
By the valuative property, it follows that for any
m\smallin M
,
f(m)
and
g(m)
specialize to the same maximal element
h(m)
.
Thus we get a zig-zag

f\leq h\geq g

so that
[f]=[g]
in
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(\mathrm{Gal}(T_{\zar}))(\mathrm{\beta}(M))
, proving injectivity.
∎

4.24 Example
.

Corollary

4.23
shows that even if
X
is a connected scheme,
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)
can be a nontrivial condensed set.
Concretely, we may take
T
to be the underlying topological space of the adic unit disk.
Then
T
is a connected spectral topological space, so there exists a ring
R
and a homeomorphism
T\simeq\lvert\mathrm{Spec}(R)\rvert
.
Thus
\mathrm{Spec}(R)
is connected but
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(\mathrm{Spec}(R))=T^{\sep}
is a nontrivial compact Hausdorff space.
In fact, this space is homeomorphic to the underlying space of the corresponding Berkovich disk (cf.
[
Huber:book
, Remark 8.3.2]
).

4.25 Remark
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme. Note that
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)
is qs.
Indeed, this is clearly true for w-contractible qcqs
X
and in general it follows by proétale covering by w-contractibles and using the following observation: let
X^{\prime}\to X
be a proétale surjection. Then the induced map of condensed sets
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X^{\prime})\to\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)
is surjective.
Indeed, using
2.7
, this eventually boils down to the statement that for a map of simplicial sets that is surjective on vertices, the induced map on
\pi_{0}
is surjective.

Theorem

4.18
can also be used to show that for a general qcqs scheme
X
, the condensed set
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)
can be quite exotic (in particular,
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)
is not generally quasiseparated in the sense of
7.17
). This is achieved in the following example.

4.26 Example

((schematic Warsaw circle))
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme with the property that any two points may be connected by a zig-zag of specializations but such that the minimal length of such a chain is not bounded by any natural number.
Then we have

\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)({\mathbin{*}})\simeq{\mathbin{*}}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

However, for any function
f\colon\mathbf{N}\to|X|
such that the minimal length of a zig-zag connecting
f(n)
and
f(0)
is at least
n
, the function
f
and the constant function at
f(0)
yield different elements in
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)(\mathrm{\beta}(\mathbf{N}))
.
Thus,
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)
is a nontrivial condensed set whose underlying set is the point and therefore not quasiseparated.
Indeed, if it were quasiseparated it would be qcqs and thus representable by a compact Hausdorff space.

Let us give a concrete example of a scheme satisfying these properties.
Fix an algebraically closed field
k
and write
{\mathbin{*}}=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
.
Let
X\smallin{\mathbin{*}}_{\proet}
be a scheme such that
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)=\mathbf{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, i.e., the converging sequence of points together with its limit.
Each connected component of
X
is just a copy of
{\mathbin{*}}
.
Take two copies
X_{1}^{+}=X_{2}^{+}=\mathbf{A}^{1}_{k}\times_{{\mathbin{*}}}X
of a scheme that, intuitively, is a sequence of affine lines converging to another affine line.
Fix two points, say
0,1
, on each copy of
\mathbf{A}^{1}_{k}
and glue
X_{1}^{+}
and
X_{2}^{+}
to obtain a
zigzag
of
\mathbf{A}_{k}^{1}
’s intersecting at
0
’s and
1
’s and converging to a copy of
\mathbf{A}_{k}^{1}
, as displayed in
Figure

1
.

Figure 1:
Constructing the scheme
X^{+}
.

Let us denote this scheme simply by
X^{+}
.
To formalize this gluing procedure, one notes that we are gluing affine schemes along closed subschemes, so by
[
Sch05
, Theorem 3.4]
the pushout exists and is also affine.

Now, this scheme satisfies the condition of having specialization-distances between points growing arbitrarily but it still needs a small correction: the points on the limit
\mathbf{A}^{1}_{k}
are not joinable by a specialization sequence with the points on the zigzag.
To amend it, add a further copy of
\mathbf{A}^{1}_{k}
joining an arbitrarily chosen pair of
k
-points of the the leftmost line of the zigzag with the limit line of
X^{+}
.
Let us denote by
X^{++}
this schematic ‘Warsaw circle’.
One can check that
X^{++}
satisfies the desired properties.

4.3
Computation:
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}
of rings of continuous functions

Let
T
be a compact Hausdorff space.
We conclude this section by using
Theorem

4.18
to compute the condensed homotopy type of the ring of continuous functions
\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{C})
; we show that it is
0
-truncated, and coincides with the condensed set represented by
T
.
We accomplish this by proving a more general result.
To state it, recall that the ring
\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{C})
has the property that every prime ideal is contained in a unique maximal ideal (see
Theorem

A.24
).
Moreover,
[
zbMATH03322176
, Chapitre VII, Proposition 4]
shows that the local rings of
\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{C})
at maximal ideals are strictly henselian.
We are able to compute the condensed homotopy types of rings satisfying these two properties.

To state our results, we first introduce some terminology.

4.27 Notation
.

Given a ring
R
, we write
\mathrm{MSpec}(R)\subset|\mathrm{Spec}(R)|
for the subset of maximal ideals, endowed with the subspace topology.

4.28 Recollection

((see
Appendix

A
))
.

A ring
R
is a
pm-ring
if every prime ideal of
R
is contained in a unique maximal ideal.
In this case, the space
\mathrm{MSpec}(R)
is compact Hausdorff.

First, we identify
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}
of an arbitrary pm-ring.

4.29 Proposition
.

Let
R
be a pm-ring.
Then there is a natural isomorphism of condensed sets

\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(\mathrm{Spec}(R))\similarrightarrow\mathrm{MSpec}(R)\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

This isomorphism is constructed in the course of the proof.

Proof.

By
Theorem

A.9
, the map of topological spaces
{|\mathrm{Spec}(R)|}\to{\mathrm{MSpec}(R)}
that sends a prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}
to the unique maximal ideal containing
\mathfrak{p}
is a continuous retraction of the inclusion.
This retraction is also continuous for the constructible topology and therefore defines a map of condensed sets

\mathrm{Map}_{\textbf{{Top}}}(-,|\mathrm{Spec}(R)|^{\cons})\to\mathrm{MSpec}(R)\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Furthermore it clearly respects the equivalence relation described in
Theorem

4.18
and therefore induces a map

\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(\mathrm{Spec}(R))\to\mathrm{MSpec}(R)\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

To check that this map is an isomorphism, it suffices to check this after evaluating at
\mathrm{\beta}(M)
for any set
M
.
Using the explicit description given in
Theorem

4.18
and the fact that
\mathrm{MSpec}(R)
is compact Hausdorff (
Corollary

A.10
), this is immediate.
∎

Under stronger hypotheses, we compute the whole condensed homotopy type:

4.30 Theorem
.

Let
R
be a pm-ring with the property that all local rings at maximal ideals are strictly henselian.
Then
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathrm{Spec}(R))
is
0
-truncated; hence there is a natural equivalence of condensed anima

{\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathrm{Spec}(R))}\similarrightarrow{\mathrm{MSpec}(R)}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

To show that
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathrm{Spec}(R))
is
0
-truncated, we use the description of the condensed homotopy type via exodromy.
We first prove some preparatory results about classifying anima of infinite products.

4.31 Lemma
.

Let
I
be a set and let
(\mathcal{C}_{i})_{i\smallin I}
be
\infty
-categories.
Assume that for each
i\smallin I
, there exists a left adjoint functor
\lambda_{i}\colon{A_{i}}\to{\mathcal{C}_{i}}
where
A_{i}
is an anima.
Then all of the maps in the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}(\textstyle\prod_{i\smallin I}A_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i\smallin I}\mathrm{B}A_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}(\textstyle\prod_{i\smallin I}\mathcal{C}_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i\smallin I}\mathrm{B}\mathcal{C}_{i}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}(\prod_{i\smallin I}\lambda_{i})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i\smallin I}\mathrm{B}\lambda_{i}}

are equivalences of anima.

Proof.

First observe that since each
\lambda_{i}
is a left adjoint, the induced functor on products

\textstyle\prod_{i\smallin I}\lambda_{i}\colon\prod_{i\smallin I}A_{i}\to\prod_{i\smallin I}\mathcal{C}_{i}

is also a left adjoint.
Since each
A_{i}
is an anima, the top horizontal map is an equivalence.
Since
\prod_{i\smallin I}\lambda_{i}
and each
\lambda_{i}
is a left adjoint and the functor
\mathrm{B}\colon{\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty}}\to{\textbf{{Ani}}}
sends left adjoints to equivalences
[
CJ24
, Corollary 2.11]
, the vertical maps are also equivalences.
Thus, by the 2-of-3 property, the bottom horizontal map is an equivalence, as desired.
∎

4.32 Example
.

Let
I
be a set and let
(\mathcal{C}_{i})_{i\smallin I}
be
\infty
-categories.
Assume that for each
i\smallin I
, each connected component of the
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}_{i}
admits an initial object.
Then the hypotheses of
Lemma

4.31
are satisfied where each
A_{i}
is the set of initial objects of connected components of
\mathcal{C}_{i}
and
\lambda_{i}
is the inclusion.
In particular,

\mathrm{B}(\textstyle\prod_{i\smallin I}\mathcal{C}_{i})\simeq\prod_{i\smallin I}\mathrm{B}\mathcal{C}_{i}

is
0
-truncated.

We also need the following criterion for detecting when a condensed anima is
0
-truncated:

4.33 Lemma
.

Let
n\geq 0
be an integer.
Then a condensed anima
A
is
n
-truncated if and only if for each set
M
, the anima
A(\mathrm{\beta}(M))
is
n
-truncated.

Proof.

Since every extremally disconnected profinite set is a retract of the Čech–Stone compactification of a set, this follows from the fact that every retract of an
n
-truncated anima is
n
-truncated.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

4.30
.

Note that, in light of
Proposition

4.29
, the final statement follows from the claim that
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathrm{Spec}(R))
is
0
-truncated; so we just show this.
Let us write
X=\mathrm{Spec}(R)
.
By
Lemma

4.33
, it suffices to show that for every set
M
, the classifying anima of the category
\mathrm{Gal}(X)(\mathrm{\beta}(M))
is
0
-truncated.
Together,
3.32
and
2.22
show that

\mathrm{Gal}(X)(\mathrm{\beta}(M))\simeq\prod_{m\smallin M}\mathrm{Gal}(X)(\{m\})\simeq\prod_{m\smallin M}\Pt(X_{\et})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

So by
Example

4.32
, it suffices to show that every connected component of
\Pt(X_{\et})
has an initial object.
This last statement is immediate from the assumption that
R
is a pm-ring and all local rings at maximal ideals are strictly henselian.
∎

We now derive some consequences of
Theorem

4.30
.
The first is a computation of the étale homotopy type of these pm-rings, which appears to be new.

4.34 Corollary
.

Let
R
be a pm-ring with the property that all local rings at maximal ideals are strictly henselian.
Then there is a canonical equivalence of proanima

{\Pi_{<\infty}^{\et}(\mathrm{Spec}(R))}\similarrightarrow{\Pi_{<\infty}(\mathrm{MSpec}(R))}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Here,
\Pi_{<\infty}(\mathrm{MSpec}(R))
denotes the shape of the compact Hausdorff space
\mathrm{MSpec}(R)
.
See
2.30
.

Proof.

We apply the functor
(-)^{\wedge}_{\disc}\colon\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})\to\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{<\infty})
to the equivalence in
Theorem

4.30
.
To conclude, note that by
Lemma

3.14
, we have

\displaystyle\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathrm{Spec}(R))^{\wedge}_{\disc}

\displaystyle\simeq\Pi_{<\infty}^{\et}(\mathrm{Spec}(R))

and by
Lemma

2.32
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{MSpec}(R)^{\wedge}_{\disc}

\displaystyle\simeq\Pi_{<\infty}(\mathrm{MSpec}(R))\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}\qed

Finally, we turn to the special case of rings of continuous functions.

4.35 Corollary
.

Let
T
be a topological space and let
\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{b}}(T,\mathbf{C})
denote the ring of bounded continuous functions to
\mathbf{C}
.
Then there are natural equivalences

\displaystyle\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathrm{Spec}(\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{b}}(T,\mathbf{C})))

\displaystyle\similarrightarrow\mathrm{\beta}(T)

and

\displaystyle\Pi_{<\infty}^{\et}(\mathrm{Spec}(\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{b}}(T,\mathbf{C})))

\displaystyle\similarrightarrow\Pi_{<\infty}(\mathrm{\beta}(T))\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

4.36
.

Note that if
T
is compact Hausdorff, then
\mathrm{\beta}(T)=T
and
\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{b}}(T,\mathbf{C})=\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{C})
.

Proof.

By the universal property of Čech–Stone compactification, the natural map
T\to\mathrm{\beta}(T)
induces an isomorphism of rings

\mathrm{C}(\mathrm{\beta}(T),\mathbf{C})\similarrightarrow\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{b}}(T,\mathbf{C})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

By
Theorem

A.24
, the ring
\mathrm{C}(\mathrm{\beta}(T),\mathbf{C})
is a pm-ring and by
Theorem

A.30
there is a natural homeomorphism
{\mathrm{\beta}(T)}\similarrightarrow{\mathrm{MSpec}(\mathrm{C}(\mathrm{\beta}(T),\mathbf{C}))}
.
Furthermore,
[
zbMATH03322176
, Chapitre VII, Proposition 4]
shows that the local rings of
\mathrm{C}(\mathrm{\beta}(T),\mathbf{C})
at maximal ideals are strictly henselian.
Thus the claim follows from
Theorems

4.30
and
4.34
applied to
R=\mathrm{C}(\mathrm{\beta}(T),\mathbf{C})
.
∎

4.37 Remark
.

Let
T
be a compact Hausdorff space that admits a CW structure and
t\smallin T
.
Since
T
admits a CW structure, the shape
\Pi_{\infty}(T)
coincides with the underlying anima of
T
.
Hence
Corollary

4.35
shows that, up to protruncation, the étale homotopy type of
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{C}))
coincides with the underlying anima of
T
.
In particular, the SGA3 étale fundamental group of
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{C}))
at the maximal ideal of functions vanishing at
t
coincides with the usual fundamental group
\mathrm{\pi}_{1}(T,t)
.

5
Fiber sequences

Let
k
be a field with separable closure
\bar{k}\supset k
, and let
X
be a qcqs
k
-scheme.
Write
X_{\bar{k}}
for the basechange of
X
to
\bar{k}
.
Then the naturally null sequence of étale homotopy types

(5.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{<\infty}^{\et}(X_{\bar{k}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{<\infty}^{\et}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\BGal_{k}}

is a fiber sequence, see
[
arXiv:2209.03476
, Theorem 0.2]
.
The existence of this fiber sequence implies the usual fundamental exact sequence for étale fundamental groups
[
STK
,
SGA 1
]
.

The first goal of this section, accomplished in
Section

5.1
, is to prove the analogue of the fundamental fiber sequence (
5.1
) for the condensed homotopy type.
The second goal of this section, accomplished in
Section

5.2
, is to show that given a smooth proper morphism of schemes
{X}\to{S}
, up to suitable completion, the homotopy-theoretic fiber of the induced map
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)\to\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(S)
agrees with the condensed homotopy type of the scheme-theoretic fiber.
See
Theorem

5.12
.

5.1
The fundamental fiber sequence for the condensed homotopy type

Using the description of
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)
as the condensed classifying anima
\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}\mathrm{Gal}(X)
, the same methods as in
[
arXiv:2209.03476
]
allow us to prove the fundamental fiber sequence for the condensed homotopy type.
The key observation is that even though
\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}
does not preserve pullbacks, it preserves pullbacks along morphisms between condensed anima.
Let us now explain this point.

5.2 Recollection
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an
\infty
-category with pullbacks and
\mathcal{D}\subset\mathcal{C}
a full subcategory such that the inclusion admits a left adjoint
L\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
.
We say that the localization
L
is
locally cartesian
if for any cospan
U\to W\leftarrow V
in
\mathcal{C}
with
U,W\smallin\mathcal{D}
, the natural map

{L(U\times_{W}V)}\to{U\times_{W}L(V)}

is an equivalence.
See
[
GK17
,
Hoy17
]
.

5.3
.

Importantly, the localization
\mathrm{B}\colon{\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty}}\to{\textbf{{Ani}}}
is locally cartesian; see
[
arXiv:2209.03476
, Example 3.4]
.

5.4 Corollary
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an
\infty
-category with finite limits and let
L\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
be a locally cartesian localization that also perserves finite products.
Then the localization
L^{\mathrm{cond}}\colon{\Cond(\mathcal{C})}\to{\Cond(\mathcal{D})}
is locally cartesian.

Proof.

By definition, the functor

L^{\mathrm{cond}}\colon{\mathrm{Fun}^{\times}(\textbf{{Extr}}^{\op},\mathcal{C})}\to{\mathrm{Fun}^{\times}(\textbf{{Extr}}^{\op},\mathcal{D})}

is given by pointwise application of
L\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
.
Since finite limits in
\Cond(\mathcal{C})
and
\Cond(\mathcal{D})
are computed pointwise, the claim follows from the assumption that the localization
L
is locally cartesian.
∎

5.5 Example
.

The localization
\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}\colon{\Cond(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})}\to{\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})}
is locally cartesian.

5.6 Corollary
.

Let
f\colon{X}\to{S}
be a morphism between qcqs schemes, and let
{\bar{s}}\to{S}
be a geometric point of
S
.
If
\dim(S)=0
, then the naturally null sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X_{\bar{s}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(S)}

is a fiber sequence in the
\infty
-category
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
.
As a consequence, given a geometric point
\bar{x}\to X_{\bar{s}}
, the induced sequence of pointed condensed sets

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{1}(X_{\bar{s}},\bar{x})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{1}(X,\bar{x})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{1}(S,\bar{s})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X_{\bar{s}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(S)}

is exact.

Proof.

For the first claim, note that by
[
arXiv:2209.03476
, Corollary 2.4]
and the fact that the functor
\Pro(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})\to\Cond(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})
preserves limits, the natural square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}(X_{\bar{s}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{s})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}(S)}

is a pullback square in
\Cond(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})
.
Moreover, since
\bar{s}
is a geometric point,
\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{s})\simeq{\mathbin{*}}
.
Since
\dim(S)=0
, by
Corollary

3.40
the condensed
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Gal}(S)
is a
1
-truncated condensed anima.
The claim now follows from
Proposition

3.38
and the fact that the localization
\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}
is locally cartesian.

To conclude, note that since
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(S)\simeq\mathrm{Gal}(S)
is
1
-truncated, the second claim follows from the first by taking homotopy condensed sets.
∎

5.7 Corollary
.

Let
k
be a field with separable closure
\bar{k}
, let
X
be a qcqs
k
-scheme, and fix a geometric point
\bar{x}\to X_{\bar{k}}
.
If
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{0}(X_{\bar{k}})=1
, then the sequence of condensed groups

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{1}(X_{\bar{k}},\bar{x})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{1}(X,\bar{x})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}

is exact.

5.8 Remark
.

By
Corollary

4.19
, the hypotheses of
Corollary

5.7
are satisfied if
X
is geometrically connected and
X_{\bar{k}}
has finitely many irreducible components.

As an application of the fundamental fiber sequence and
Corollary

4.35
, we compute of the condensed homotopy type of rings of continuous functions to
\mathbf{R}
:

5.9 Corollary
.

Let
T
be a compact Hausdorff space.
Then there is a natural equivalence of condensed anima

\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathrm{Spec}(\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{R})))\simeq T\times\BGal_{\mathbf{R}}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Proof.

As explained in
Lemma

A.25
, the natural ring homomorphism
\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{R})\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{C}\to\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{C})
is an isomorphism.
Hence by the fundamental fiber sequence

\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathrm{Spec}(\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{C})))\to\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathrm{Spec}(\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{R})))\to\BGal_{\mathbf{R}}

of
Corollary

5.6
, we just have to show that action of
\mathrm{Gal}_{\mathbf{R}}
on
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathrm{Spec}(\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{C})))
is trivial.
By
Theorem

4.30
, we have natural identifications

\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathrm{Spec}(\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{C})))\simeq\mathrm{MSpec}(\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{C}))\simeq T\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Thus it suffices to show that map on maximal spectra

\mathrm{MSpec}(\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{C}))\to\mathrm{MSpec}(\mathrm{C}(T,\mathbf{C}))

induced by complex conjugation is the identity.
To see this, note that by
Theorem

A.30
, each maximal ideal is given by all functions
T\to\mathbf{C}
that vanish at some fixed
t\smallin T
, and a function vanishes at a point if and only if its conjugate does.
∎

5.2
Geometric and homotopy-theoretic fibers

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a smooth and proper morphism of schemes.
The goal of this subsection is is to show that, up to suitable completion, the homotopy-theoretic fiber of the induced map
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(f)\colon\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)\to\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(S)
agrees with the condensed homotopy type of the scheme-theoretic fiber.

5.10 Notation
.

For a morphism of schemes
f\colon X\to S
and a geometric point
\bar{s}\to S
, we denote by

X_{(\bar{s})}\coloneq X\times_{S}S_{(\bar{s})}

the
Milnor ball of
f
at
\bar{s}
.
Here
S_{(\bar{s})}
denotes the strict localization at
\bar{s}
.

5.11 Recollection

((
\Sigma
-completion))
.

Let
\Sigma
be a nonempty set of prime numbers.

(1)

We write
\textbf{{Ani}}_{\Sigma}\subset\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}}
for the full subcategory spanned by those
\mathrm{\pi}
-finite anima all of whose homotopy groups are
\Sigma
-groups (i.e., their order is a product of elements of
\Sigma
).

(2)

The inclusion
\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\Sigma})\hookrightarrow\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}})
admits a left adjoint
(-)_{\Sigma}^{\wedge}
that we refer to as
\Sigma
-completion
.

(3)

We also write
(-)_{\Sigma}^{\wedge}\colon\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})\to\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\Sigma})
for the left adjoint of the inclusion

\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\Sigma})\hookrightarrow\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}})\hookrightarrow\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

As a consequence of the exodromy description of the condensed homotopy type, we can apply a profinite version of Quillen’s Theorem B, see
Section

B.2
, to prove:

5.12 Theorem
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a smooth and proper morphism between qcqs schemes and let
\bar{s}\to S
be a geometric point.
Let
\Sigma
be a nonempty set of primes invertible on
S
.
Then the induced map

\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X_{\bar{s}})\to\textup{fib}_{\bar{s}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(f))

becomes an equivalence after completion with respect to
\Sigma
.

Proof.

We want to apply
Theorem

B.7
to the functor
\mathrm{Gal}(f)\colon\mathrm{Gal}(X)\to\mathrm{Gal}(S)
induced by
f
.
To verify that the assumptions of
Theorem

B.7
are satisfied, we need to see that for any specialization
\eta\colon\bar{t}^{\prime}\to\bar{t}
in
S
, the induced map

(5.13)

\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathrm{Gal}(X)_{\bar{t}/})\to\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}(\mathrm{Gal}(X)_{\bar{t}^{\prime}/})

becomes an equivalence after
\Sigma
-completion.

Recall that by
[
BGH20
, Corollary 12.4.5]
, we have a natural equivalence of underlying
\infty
-categories

(5.14)

\mathrm{Gal}(S_{(\bar{t})})\similarrightarrow\mathrm{Gal}(S)_{\bar{t}/}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Using
6.5
below, one can show that this equivalence refines to an equivalence of condensed
\infty
-categories, see
[
Wol25
, Proposition 7.3.3.7]
for more details.
Furthermore,
[
HHW24a
, Proposition 2.4]
implies, that the natural functor

\mathrm{Gal}(X_{(\bar{t})})\to\mathrm{Gal}(X)_{\bar{t}/}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}

induced by the equivalence (
5.14
), is an equivalence of condensed
\infty
-categories as well.
Thus by
Lemma

3.14
, the
\Sigma
-completion of the map (
5.13
) identifies with the specialization map

\widehat{\Pi}_{\infty}^{\et}(X_{(\bar{t})})_{\Sigma}^{\wedge}\to\widehat{\Pi}_{\infty}^{\et}(X_{(\bar{t}^{\prime})})_{\Sigma}^{\wedge}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

By
[
arXiv:2304.00938
, Proposition 2.49]
, this specialization map is an equivalence.
Thus,
Theorem

B.7
implies that the natural map
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X_{(\bar{s})})\to\textup{fib}_{\bar{s}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(f))
becomes an equivalence after
\Sigma
-completion.
Finally, note that by
Lemma

3.14
and
[
HHW24
, Corollary 2.39]
, the natural map

\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X_{\bar{s}})\to\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X_{(\bar{s})})

becomes an equivalence after
\Sigma
-completion.
∎

5.15 Remark
.

In the setting of
Theorem

5.12
, the canonical map
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X_{\bar{s}})\to\textup{fib}_{\bar{s}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(f))
is not generally an equivalence before
\Sigma
-completion.
The reason why this fails is that the proper and smooth basechange theorems do not hold for arbitrary proétale sheaves; they only hold for constructible étale sheaves.

5.16 Remark
.

Theorem

5.12
is an analogue of Friedlander’s result
[
Fri73a
, Theorem 3.7]
.
Since we do not have to require that the base
S
be normal, at the cost of working with a more complicated homotopy type, our result holds in a more general setup.
However, since the
\Sigma
-completion functor does not preserve fiber sequences, it is also not immediate how to recover Friedlander’s result from ours.

6
Integral Descent

The goal of this section is to prove that the condensed homotopy type satisfies integral hyperdescent.
Let us start by formulating what we mean by this more precisely.

6.1 Definition
.

Let
X
be a scheme and
\mathcal{C}
an
\infty
-category.

(1)

We call an augmented simplical object
X_{\bullet}\to X
an
integral hypercover
if for each
n\geq 0
, the morphism
X_{n}\to X
is integral and
X_{0}\to X
and
X_{n}\to(\cosk_{n-1}(X_{\bullet}))_{n}
are surjective.

(2)

We call a functor
F\colon\textbf{{Sch}}^{\mathrm{qcqs}}\to\mathcal{C}
a
hypercomplete integral cosheaf
if
F
sends integral hypercovers to colimit diagrams.

The main goal of
Section

6.1
is to show that
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(-)
is a hypercomplete integral cosheaf, which we achieve in
Corollary

6.16
.
In fact, our methods will show that already
\mathrm{Gal}(-)
is a hypercomplete integral cosheaf of condensed categories.
In
Section

6.2
, we use some of the results in this section to characterize those morphisms of schemes, for which the étale
\infty
-topos is compatible with basechange; this included integral morphisms.

6.1
Integral morphisms and right fibrations

In this subsection, we show that for an integral morphism of schemes, the induced functor on Galois categories is a right fibration of condensed categories.
We begin by recalling the notion of a right fibration of condensed
\infty
-categories:

6.2 Definition
.

We say that a functor of condensed
\infty
-categories
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
is a
right fibration
if and only if the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cond}}([1],\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cond}}([1],\mathcal{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\circ-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

is a cartesian square in
\Cond(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})
.

6.3 Remark
.

Definition

6.2
is a special case of the notion of a right fibration of simplicial objects in a general
\infty
-topos
\mathcal{B}
, as introduced in
[
Mar21
, Definition 4.1.1]
.
In particular it follows from the discussion in
loc. cit.
that right fibrations in
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op},\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}}))
are the right class in an orthogonal factorization system.
The left class consists of the
final
maps, i.e., the smallest saturated class which contains all maps of the form
\{n\}\times S\hookrightarrow[n]\times S
for
n\smallin\mathbf{N}
and
S\smallin\ProFin
.
See
[
Mar21
, Lemma 4.1.2]
.

6.4 Remark
.

A functor
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
of condensed
\infty
-categories is a right fibration if and only if for every profinite set
S
, the functor
f(S)\colon\mathcal{C}(S)\to\mathcal{D}(S)
is a right fibration of
\infty
-categories.
Indeed, the square in
Definition

6.2
is cartesian if and only if this is true after evaluation at every profinite set
S
.
Under the equivalence
\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cond}}([1],\mathcal{C})(S)\simeq\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathcal{C}(S))
, the claim then follows by the characterization of right fibrations via a corresponding cartesian square, see
[
Cis19
, Proposition 3.4.5]
.

In the cases we care about, being a right fibration can often be detected on the level of underlying
\infty
-categories, which we deduce from the following observation.

6.5 Observation
.

Recall from
[
SAG
,
Theorem E.3.1.6
]
that the functor

\lim\colon\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}})\to\textbf{{Ani}}

is conservative.
It follows that the functor
\lim_{*}\colon\mathrm{Cat}(\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}}))\to\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty}
given by postcomposition with
\lim
is also conservative.

6.6 Lemma
.

Let
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a functor in
\mathrm{Cat}(\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}}))
considered as a functor of condensed
\infty
-categories.
If the underlying functor of
\infty
-categories is a right fibration, then
f
is a right fibration of condensed
\infty
-categories.

Proof.

By definition,
f
is a right fibration if and only if the induced map

(6.7)

\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cond}}([1],\mathcal{C})\to\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cond}}([1],\mathcal{D})\times_{\mathcal{D}}\mathcal{C}

is an equivalence of condensed
\infty
-categories.
Since
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
are in
\mathrm{Cat}(\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}}))
, it follows that
\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cond}}([1],\mathcal{C})
and
\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cond}}([1],\mathcal{D})
are also in
\mathrm{Cat}(\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}}))
.
Thus, by
6.5
, the comparison map (
6.7
) is an equivalence if and only if it an equivalence on underlying
\infty
-categories.
Since taking underlying
\infty
-categories commutes with pullbacks, this proves the claim.
∎

By
3.32
, we immediately deduce the following.

6.8 Corollary
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a morphism of qcqs schemes.
Then the induced functor

\mathrm{Gal}(f)\colon\!\mathrm{Gal}(X)\to\mathrm{Gal}(Y)

is a right fibration of condensed categories if and only if this is true on the underlying categories.

6.9 Proposition
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be an integral morphism of qcqs schemes.
Then the induced functor

\mathrm{Gal}(f)\colon\mathrm{Gal}(X)\to\mathrm{Gal}(Y)

is a right fibration of condensed categories.

Proof.

By
Corollary

6.8
, it suffices to check this on underlying categories.
The statement about underlying categories appears in
[
BGH20
, Proposition 14.1.6]
; for the convenience of the reader, we give a quick proof here.

Throughout the proof, we simply write
\mathrm{Gal}(-)
for the underlying category as well.
By
[
STK
,
Tag 09YZ
]
, any integral morphism
f\colon X\to Y
with
Y
qcqs can be written as
f=\lim_{i}f_{i}
for some cofiltered system of
finite
morphisms
f_{i}\colon X_{i}\to Y
.
Since right fibrations are stable under limits, by the continuity of étale
\infty
-topoi
[
SGA 4ii
,
CM21
]
, we may assume that
f
is finite.
Since
\mathrm{Gal}(X)
and
\mathrm{Gal}(Y)
are
1
-categories, by
[
Ker
,
Tag 015H
]
it suffices to show that any lifting problem of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{1\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}(Y).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\exists!}?}

has a
unique
solution.
Writing
\bar{y}
for the source of the map
s
, this diagram factors as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{1\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}(Y)_{\bar{y}/}\times_{\mathrm{Gal}(Y)}\mathrm{Gal}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}(Y)_{\bar{y}/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}(Y)\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\exists!}?}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

and it suffices to show that this induced lifting problem has a unique solution.

By
[
BGH20
, Corollary 12.4.5]
and
[
HHW24a
, Corollary 2.4]
, we can identify

\mathrm{Gal}(Y)_{\bar{y}/}\simeq\mathrm{Gal}(Y_{(\bar{y})})\text{\hskip 23.49976ptand\hskip 23.49976pt}\mathrm{Gal}(X)\times_{\mathrm{Gal}(Y)}\mathrm{Gal}(Y_{(\bar{y})})\simeq\mathrm{Gal}(X\times_{Y}Y_{(\bar{y})})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Moreover, since
f\colon X\to Y
is finite, by
[
STK
,
Tag 04GH
]
we have a coproduct decomposition
X\times_{Y}Y_{(\bar{y})}=\coprod_{\bar{x}_{i}\smallin f^{-1}(\bar{y})}X_{(\bar{x}_{i})}
.
Now the map

\{1\}\to\mathrm{Gal}(Y_{(\bar{y})})\times_{\mathrm{Gal}(Y)}\mathrm{Gal}(X)\simeq\coprod_{i}\mathrm{Gal}(X_{(\bar{x}_{i})})

factors through
\mathrm{Gal}(X_{(\bar{x}_{i_{0}})})
for some
i_{0}
.
Hence, writing
\bar{x}\coloneq\bar{x}_{i_{0}}
, we finally arrive at a lifting problem of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{1\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}(X_{(\bar{x})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}(Y_{(\bar{y})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}(Y).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\exists!}?}

Here, existence and uniqueness of a lift is clear.
Let
\bar{y}^{\prime}
be the target of the map
s
, determined by
\{1\}\to\mathrm{Gal}(X_{(\bar{x})})
.
Note that
\bar{x}
is the initial object of
\mathrm{Gal}(X_{(\bar{x})})\simeq\mathrm{Gal}(X)_{\bar{x}/}
, and also the only object lifting
\bar{y}
.
So if there exists a lift, it has to be the unique map from
\bar{x}\to\bar{x}^{\prime}
for
\bar{x}^{\prime}
the lift of
\bar{y}^{\prime}
.
Since
\bar{y}
is the initial object of
\mathrm{Gal}(Y_{(\bar{y})})\simeq\mathrm{Gal}(Y)_{\bar{y}/}
, it is clear that
\bar{x}\to\bar{x}^{\prime}
actually lifts the map
s\colon\bar{y}\to\bar{y}^{\prime}
we started with.
∎

6.10 Corollary

((Künneth formula for integral morphisms))
.

Let
X\to Y
be an integral morphism of qcqs schemes.
Then for any qcqs scheme
Y^{\prime}
and morphism
Y^{\prime}\to Y
the natural functor

\mathrm{Gal}(X\times_{Y}Y^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Gal}(X)\times_{\mathrm{Gal}(Y)}\mathrm{Gal}(Y^{\prime})

is an equivalence.

Proof.

As integral morphisms and right fibrations are stable under pullbacks, by
Proposition

6.9
both functors

\mathrm{Gal}(\pr_{1})\colon\mathrm{Gal}(X\times_{Y}Y^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Gal}(Y^{\prime})\text{\hskip 23.49976ptand\hskip 23.49976pt}\pr_{1}\colon\mathrm{Gal}(X)\times_{\mathrm{Gal}(Y)}\mathrm{Gal}(Y^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Gal}(Y^{\prime})

are right fibrations.
Therefore, by
[
Ker
,
Tag 01VE
]
it suffices to see that the natural functor

\mathrm{Gal}(X\times_{Y}Y^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Gal}(X)\times_{\mathrm{Gal}(Y)}\mathrm{Gal}(Y^{\prime})

becomes an equivalence after taking fibers over any
\bar{y}^{\prime}\smallin\mathrm{Gal}(Y^{\prime})
.
This holds by
[
HHW24
, Corollary 2.4]
.
∎

6.11 Lemma
.

Let
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a morphism in
\mathrm{Cat}(\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}}))
.
Then
f
is surjective as a functor of condensed
\infty
-categories (i.e., for all
S\smallin\textbf{{Extr}}
, the functor
\mathcal{C}(S)\to\mathcal{D}(S)
is surjective) if and only if the induced functor on underlying
\infty
-categories
f({\mathbin{*}})\colon\mathcal{C}({\mathbin{*}})\to\mathcal{D}({\mathbin{*}})
is surjective.

6.12 Observation
.

The inclusion
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})\to\Cond(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})
also admits a right adjoint.
We denote this right adjoint by
(-)^{\simeq}
.

Proof of
Lemma

6.11
.

First, by definition, if
f
is a surjective functor of condensed
\infty
-categories, then
f({\mathbin{*}})\colon\mathcal{C}({\mathbin{*}})\to\mathcal{D}({\mathbin{*}})
is surjective.
Conversely, if
f({\mathbin{*}})\colon\mathcal{C}({\mathbin{*}})\to\mathcal{D}({\mathbin{*}})
is surjective, then it follows from
[
SAG
,
Corollary E.4.6.3
]
that the induced map
\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}\to\mathcal{D}^{\simeq}
is an effective epimorphism in
\Pro(\textbf{{Ani}}_{\mathrm{\pi}})\subset\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
.
Now let
S\smallin\textbf{{Extr}}
.
Since any map
S\to\mathcal{D}
in
\Cond(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})
factors through
\mathcal{D}^{\simeq}
and
S
is projective in
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
it follows that we can find a lift in the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\simeq}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

which completes the proof.
∎

6.13 Corollary
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a surjective morphism of qcqs schemes.
Then the functor of condensed categories
\mathrm{Gal}(f)\colon\mathrm{Gal}(X)\to\mathrm{Gal}(Y)
is surjective.

Proof of
Corollary

6.13
.

By
Lemma

6.11
, we just need to see that the induced functor on categories of points
\mathrm{Gal}(X)({\mathbin{*}})\to\mathrm{Gal}(Y)({\mathbin{*}})
is surjective.
Since any point of
X_{\et}
is represented by a geometric point
\bar{x}\to X
, it is clear.
∎

Right fibrations automatically satisfy descent in the following sense:

6.14 Definition
.

An augmented simplicial
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}_{\bullet}\to\mathcal{C}
is a
hypercover
if for each
n\smallin\mathbf{N}
, the induced functor
\mathcal{C}_{n}\to(\cosk_{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{\bullet}))_{n}
is surjective.

6.15 Lemma
.

Let
\mathcal{C}_{\bullet}\to\mathcal{C}
be a hypercover in
\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty}
, and assume that for each
n\smallin\mathbf{N}
, the induced functor
\mathcal{C}_{n}\to\mathcal{C}
is a right fibration.
Then
\colim_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\op}}\mathcal{C}_{\bullet}\similarrightarrow\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

By straightening-unstraightening, our given hypercover translates to a hypercover of the terminal object in the
\infty
-category
\RFib(\mathcal{C})\simeq\PSh(\mathcal{C})
of right fibrations over
\mathcal{C}
.
Furthermore, the inclusion
\RFib(\mathcal{C})\subset\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty,/\mathcal{C}}
preserves limits and colimits (the case of limits is clear as right fibrations are defined via a lifting property, for colimits see
[
Ram22
, Corollary A.5]
).
Since
\RFib(\mathcal{C})
is a presheaf
\infty
-topos and therefore hypercomplete, the claim follows.
∎

We can now deduce the desired descent results.

6.16 Corollary
.

(1)

The functor
\mathrm{Gal}\colon\textbf{{Sch}}^{\mathrm{qcqs}}\to\Cond(\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty})
is a hypercomplete integral cosheaf.

(2)

The functor
(-)_{\proet}^{\hyp}\colon(\textbf{{Sch}}^{\mathrm{qcqs}})^{\op}\to\textbf{{Cat}}_{\infty}
with functoriality given by pullbacks is an integral hypersheaf.

(3)

The functor
