is a (semistable) curve over a separably closed field
k
, with
G_{i}=\mathrm{\pi}_{1}^{\et}(X^{\nu}_{i},\bar{x}_{i})
, where
X=\coprod_{i}X^{\nu}_{i}
is the the normalization of
X
.

For
\mathrm{\pi}_{1}^{\et}
(or even
\mathrm{\pi}^{\proet}_{1}
), this is a classical computation using the van Kampen theorem when
X
is semistable.
See
[
Stix:vanKampen
, Example 5.5]
in the case of
\mathrm{\pi}_{1}^{\et}
or
[
Lav18
, Theorem 1.17]
for
\mathrm{\pi}^{\proet}_{1}
.
With some care, this can be done for arbitrary curves, see
[
larayuzhang2022theoremmeromorphicdescentspecialization
, Theorem 2.27]
.
A similar computation (using
Theorem

7.51
) should extend this to
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond},\qs}_{1}
.

8
Noohi completion of the condensed fundamental group

Let
X
be a topologically noetherian scheme.
The goal of this section is to recover the proétale fundamental group
\mathrm{\pi}^{\proet}_{1}(X,\bar{x})
of
[
BhattScholzeProEtale
, §7]
from the condensed fundamental group
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{1}(X,\bar{x})
.
The main input needed for this is the observation that all
weakly locally constant sheaves
in the sense of
[
BhattScholzeProetale
, Definition 7.3.1]
can be recovered from
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{1}(X,\bar{x})
.
We prove a stronger derived version of that result in
Section

8.1
.
In
Section

8.2
, we explain how to Noohi complete condensed groups and show that the Noohi completion of
\mathrm{\pi}^{\mathrm{cond}}_{1}(X,\bar{x})
is indeed the proétale fundamental group.
See
Theorem

8.17
.

8.1
Recovering weakly locally constant sheaves

In this subsection, we explain how to recover weakly locally constant proétale sheaves on a scheme
X
as representations of the condensed homotopy type.
The following is a generalization of
[
BhattScholzeProetale
, Definition 7.3.1]
to sheaves of anima:

8.1 Recollection
.

Recall that for a qcqs scheme
X
there is a canonical algebraic morphism
\Sh(\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X))\to X_{\et}
induced by sending a clopen subset of
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(X)
to its preimage in
X
.
Furthermore, we say that
F\smallin X_{\proet}^{\hyp}
is
locally weakly constant
if there is a proétale cover
\{U_{i}\to X\}_{i\smallin I}
by qcqs schemes such that each
F|_{U_{i}}
is in the image of the canonical algebraic morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sh(\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(U_{i}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{i,\et}^{\hyp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{\hyp}_{i,\proet}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{{\mathbin{*}}}}

We write
\wLoc(X)\subset X_{\proet}^{\hyp}
for the full subcategory spanned by the locally weakly constant sheaves.

We want to show that
\wLoc(X)
is equivalent to the
\infty
-category of continuous functors from
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)
into the following condensed subcategory of
\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Ani}})
.

8.2 Definition
.

We define the condensed
\infty
-category
\textbf{{Ani}}{}^{\ult}
by the assignment

S\mapsto\Sh(S)

for every profinite set
S
.
7
7

7

The fact that
\textbf{{Ani}}{}^{\ult}
satisfies descent for surjections of profinite sets follows from the proper basechange theorem.
See
[
Hai22
, Theorem 0.5 & Example 1.28]
.

Similarly, we refer to the
0
-truncated version of this condensed
\infty
-category by
\textbf{{Set}}{}^{\ult}
.

8.3 Recollection
.

Let
S
be a profinite set, and write
c^{{\mathbin{*}}}_{S}\colon\PSh(S)\to\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})_{/S}
for the left Kan extension of the natural functor

\Open(S)\hookrightarrow\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})_{/S}

along the Yoneda emebdding.
Then the restriction

c_{S}^{*}\colon\Sh(S)\to\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})_{/S}

is a fully faithful left exact left adjoint.
See
[
Hai22
, §3.2 & Corollary 4.9]
.
Moreover, this comparison functor is natural in
S

[
Hai22
, Lemma 3.16]
, hence induces a fully faithful functor of condensed
\infty
-categories

\textbf{{Ani}}{}^{\ult}\hookrightarrow\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Ani}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

8.4 Remark
.

The superscript ‘ult’ comes from the word
ultrastructure
.
Any category with filtered colimits and infinite products can be canonically upgraded to an ultracategory by equipping it with the
categorical ultrastructure
, see
[
Lur18
, Example 1.3.8]
.
In
[
Lur18
, Construction 4.1.1]
Lurie explains how to regard ultracategories as condensed categories.
Furthermore it follows from
[
Lur18
, Theorem 3.4.4]
that the image of
Set
equipped with the categorical ultrastructure is precisely
\textbf{{Set}}{}^{\ult}
.

8.5 Recollection
.

By
[
Wol22
, Corollary 1.2]
, precomposition with the localization functor
b\colon\mathrm{Gal}(X)\to\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cond}}\mathrm{Gal}(X)=\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X)
induces a fully faithful functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b^{*}\colon\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X),\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Ani}})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}\big\lparen\mathrm{Gal}(X),\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Ani}})\big\rparen\simeq X_{\proet}^{\hyp}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}}

Cf. the proof of
Proposition

3.38
.

8.6 Theorem
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme.
The composite fully faithful functor

(8.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X),\textbf{{Ani}}{}^{\ult}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X),\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Ani}})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\proet}^{\hyp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b^{*}}

has image the full subcategory
\wLoc(X)
of locally weakly constant sheaves.

The idea of the proof is to show it first in the case of w-contractible schemes, then conclude by proétale hyperdescent.

8.8 Lemma
.

Let
W
be a w-contractible scheme.
Then the fully faithful functor

\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(W),\textbf{{Ani}}{}^{\ult})\to W^{\hyp}_{\proet}

has image
\wLoc(W)
.

Proof.

Recall from
Example

3.11
that since
W
is w-contractible,
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(W)\simeq\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(W)
.
Moreover, since
\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(W)
is a profinite set, the Yoneda lemma implies that

\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(W),\textbf{{Ani}}{}^{\ult})\simeq\textbf{{Ani}}{}^{\ult}(\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(W))\simeq\Sh(\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(W))

and the given functor is identified with the functor

\Sh(\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(W))\hookrightarrow W^{\hyp}_{\proet}

given by pullback along
W\to\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(W)
.
Therefore it lands in
\wLoc(W)
by definition; it remains to show surjectivity.

To show surjectivity, let
F\smallin\wLoc(W)
.
Then there is a proétale cover
p\colon U\to W
such that
p^{{\mathbin{*}}}(F)
is in the image of
\Sh(\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(U))\to U_{\proet}^{\hyp}
.
Since
W
is w-contractible, we can pick a section
s\colon W\to U
of
p
.
Since the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(W)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(U)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\pi}_{0}(s)}

commutes, we see that
F=s^{{\mathbin{*}}}p^{{\mathbin{*}}}(F)
is in the image of
\nu^{*}
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

8.6
.

As we have a chain of fully faithful functors (
8.7
), we regard

\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(X),\textbf{{Ani}}{}^{\ult}\big)

as a full subcategory of
X_{\proet}^{\hyp}
.
It remains to show that this full subcategory agrees with the full subcategory
\wLoc(X)
.
Since the assignment
{Y}\mapsto{\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(Y)}
is a hypercomplete proétale cosheaf, the assignment

Y\mapsto\mathrm{Fun}(\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathrm{cond}}(Y),\textbf{{Ani}}{}^{\ult})

is in a fact a subsheaf of the proétale hypersheaf
Y\mapsto Y_{\proet}^{\hyp}
.
Furthermore, by definition, the assignment

Y\mapsto\wLoc(Y)

is subsheaf of the proétale hypersheaf
Y\mapsto Y_{\proet}^{\hyp}
.
Since w-contractible schemes form a basis for the proétale topology, it suffices to see that they agree on w-contractibles, which is the content of
Lemma

8.8
.
∎

8.2
Recovering the proétale fundamental group

The goal of this subsection is to show that the
Noohi completion
of the condensed fundamental group recovers the proétale fundamental group.
Since the proétale fundamental group is a topological group, we first need to explain some technical points about the relationship between topological groups and condensed groups.

8.9 Recollection
.

The canonical functor
\mathrm{Grp}(\textbf{{Top}})\to\Cond(\textbf{{Grp}})
from topological groups to condensed groups admits a left adjoint

(-)^{\mathrm{top}}\colon\Cond(\textbf{{Grp}})\to\mathrm{Grp}(\textbf{{Top}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Note, however, that in general it is not the restriction of the left adjoint “underlying topological space” functor

(-)({\mathbin{*}})_{\mathrm{top}}\colon\Cond(\textbf{{Set}})\to\textbf{{Top}}

to condensed groups, as the latter functor does not preserve products.

It turns out that
(-)^{\mathrm{top}}
can be described as the composite of
(-)({\mathbin{*}})_{\mathrm{top}}
with the left adjoint of the inclusion of topological groups into
quasitopological groups
.

8.10 Recollection
.

A
quasitopological group
is a topological space
G
with an abstract group structure such that:

(1)

The inversion operation
G\to G
given by
g\mapsto g^{-1}
is continuous.

(2)

For each
h\smallin G
, the translation maps
G\to G
given by
g\mapsto gh
and
g\mapsto hg
are continuous.

The embedding
\mathrm{Grp}(\textbf{{Top}})\subset\textbf{{qTopGrp}}
of topological groups into quasitopological groups admits a left adjoint

\tau\colon\textbf{{qTopGrp}}\to\mathrm{Grp}(\textbf{{Top}})

that moreover preserves the underlying abstract group and only affects the topology
[
Brazas:Fundamental_group_as_topological2013
, Lemma 3.2 & Theorem 3.8]
.

While the functor
(-)({\mathbin{*}})_{\mathrm{top}}
does not provide an adjoint between
\Cond(\textbf{{Grp}})
and
\mathrm{Grp}(\textbf{{Top}})
, its image still lands in
qTopGrp
.
This is essentially because the condition of continuity of the inversion and translation maps does not involve forming a product.
That is, we have a functor

(-)({\mathbin{*}})_{\mathrm{top}}\colon\Cond(\textbf{{Grp}})\to\textbf{{qTopGrp}}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Postcomposing with
\tau
, we get a functor

\tau\circ(-)({\mathbin{*}})_{\mathrm{top}}\colon\Cond(\textbf{{Grp}})\to\mathrm{Grp}(\textbf{{Top}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

One can then quite directly verify the following:

8.11 Lemma

((see
[
Mai25
, Proposition 1.3.16]
for details))
.

The composite
\tau\circ(-)({\mathbin{*}})_{\mathrm{top}}
is left adjoint to the “associated condensed group” functor.
Visually,

\tau\circ(-)({\mathbin{*}})_{\mathrm{top}}\colon\Cond(\textbf{{Grp}})\rightleftarrows\mathrm{Grp}(\textbf{{Top}})\colon\underline{(-)}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Said differently,
(-)^{\mathrm{top}}\simeq\tau\circ(-)({\mathbin{*}})_{\mathrm{top}}
.

8.12
.

It follows from this discussion that for
G\smallin\Cond(\textbf{{Grp}})
, the abstract group
G({\mathbin{*}})
and the underlying group of
G^{\mathrm{top}}
coincide.

Before proceeding further, we provide a description of the category of
G^{\mathrm{top}}
-sets purely in terms of condensed mathematics.

8.13 Lemma
.

Let
G
be a condensed group with condensed classifying anima
\mathrm{B}G
, i.e., the condensed groupoid that sends an extremally disconnected set
S
to the one object groupoid with automorphisms
G(S)
.
There is a natural equivalence of categories

\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{B}G,\textbf{{Set}}{}^{\ult})\similarrightarrow G^{\mathrm{top}}\text{-}\textbf{{Set}}

that is compatible with the forgetful functors to
Set
.

Proof.

We first prove the following: the category
\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{B}G,\textbf{{Set}}{}^{\ult})
is equivalent to the category of pairs
(M,\alpha)
where
M\smallin\textbf{{Set}}
and
\alpha\colon G\to\underline{\Aut(M)}
is a map of condensed groups.
Here,
\Aut(M)
is the group of automorphisms of
M
equipped with the compact-open topology.
A map
(M,\alpha)\to(N,\beta)
is given by a map of sets
f\colon M\to N
such that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\Aut(M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\Aut(N)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\Hom_{\textbf{{Top}}}(M,N)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{f_{{\mathbin{*}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{f^{{\mathbin{*}}}}}

commutes (here
\Hom_{\textbf{{Top}}}(M,N)
is again given the compact-open topology).
If this description holds, the claim follows:
by the adjunction between condensed sets and topological spaces and
8.10
, the homomorphisms
\alpha
and
\beta
correspond to unique homomorphisms of quasitopological groups
\alpha^{\prime}\colon G({\mathbin{*}})_{\mathrm{top}}\to\Aut(M)
and
\beta^{\prime}\colon G({\mathbin{*}})_{\mathrm{top}}\to\Aut(N)
making the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G({\mathbin{*}})_{\mathrm{top}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Aut(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Aut(N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{\textbf{{Top}}}(M,N)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{{\mathbin{*}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{{\mathbin{*}}}}

commute.
Again, by adjunction,
Lemma

8.11
, and
8.12
, the homomorphisms
\alpha^{\prime}
and
\beta^{\prime}
correspond to unique homomorphisms of topological groups
\alpha^{\prime\prime}\colon G^{\mathrm{top}}\to\Aut(M)
and
\beta^{\prime\prime}\colon G^{\mathrm{top}}\to\Aut(N)
making the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\mathrm{top}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Aut(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Aut(N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{\textbf{{Top}}}(M,N)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{{\mathbin{*}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{{\mathbin{*}}}}

commute.
Thus the assignment

\displaystyle\left(M,\alpha\colon G\to\underline{\Aut(M)}\right)

\displaystyle\mapsto\left(M,\alpha^{\prime\prime}\colon G^{\mathrm{top}}\to\Aut(M)\right)

defines an equivalence of categories

\displaystyle\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{B}G,\textbf{{Set}}{}^{\ult})

\displaystyle\similarrightarrow G^{\mathrm{top}}\text{-}\textbf{{Set}}

as desired.

Now we prove that
\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{B}G,\textbf{{Set}}{}^{\ult})
admits the above description.
The fully faithful functor
\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{B}G,\textbf{{Set}}{}^{\ult})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{B}G,\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Set}}))
fits into a cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{B}G,\textbf{{Set}}{}^{\ult})}
Set
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{B}G,\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Set}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cond(\textbf{{Set}})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev_{*}}

where the horizontal arrows are given by pullback along
{\mathbin{*}}\to\mathrm{B}G
.
Indeed, this follows from the fact that the functors

\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(-,\textbf{{Set}}{}^{\ult}),\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(-,\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Set}}))\colon\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})^{\op}\to\textbf{{Cat}}_{1}

are sheaves and
{\mathbin{*}}\to\mathrm{B}G
is a cover in
\Cond(\textbf{{Ani}})
.
Now recall that by
[
Wol22
, Corollary 3.20]
, for a condensed set
A
, there is a natural equivalence of categories

\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(A,\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Set}}))\simeq\Cond(\textbf{{Set}})_{/A}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Using this combined with
[
arXiv:2207.09256
, Proposition A.1]
and applying
\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(-,\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Set}}))
to the Čech nerve of
{\mathbin{*}}\to\mathrm{B}G
, we obtain an equivalence

\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{B}G,\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Set}}))\simeq\lim\left(\Cond(\textbf{{Set}})\rightrightarrows\Cond(\textbf{{Set}})_{/G}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{$\rightarrow$}\vskip 0.25835pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}\vskip 0.25835pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}}\Cond(\textbf{{Set}})_{/G\times G}\right)\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

Explicitly unwinding the descent data, we see that
\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{B}G,\textbf{{Cond}}(\textbf{{Set}}))
is equivalent to the usual category of condensed sets with an action by the condensed group
G
.
In other words, its objects are condensed sets
A
together with a map
G\to\underline{\Aut}(A)
of condensed groups and the maps are defined as above.
Here
\underline{\Aut}(A)
is the maximal condensed subgroup of the condensed monoid
\underline{\Hom}(A,A)
given by the internal hom in
\Cond(\textbf{{Set}})
.
Thus, the proof will be complete if for a set
M
, we can show that there is a canonical isomorphism

\underline{\Aut}(M)\similarrightarrow\underline{\Aut(M)}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

For this, we observe that we have a canonical isomorphism

\underline{\Hom}(M,M)\simeq\underline{\Hom_{\textbf{{Top}}}(M,M)}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {,}\hss}

under which the corresponding condensed subgroups of automorphisms agree.
This completes the proof.
∎

In order to prove the main result of this section, we recall a bit about Noohi groups.

8.14 Recollection

(
[
BS15
, §7.1]
)
.

For a topological group
G
, let
F_{G}\colon G\text{-}\textbf{{Set}}\to\textbf{{Set}}
denote the forgetful functor from the category of sets equipped with a continuous
G
-action to the category of sets.
We say
G
is
Noohi
if the canonical continuous map

G\to\Aut(F_{G})

is a homeomorphism of groups.
Here,
\Aut(F_{G})
is topologized using the compact-open topology on groups
\Aut(F_{G}(M))
for
M\smallin G\text{-}\textbf{{Set}}
.
We write
\textbf{{Grp}}^{{\operatorname{Noohi}}}\subset\mathrm{Grp}(\textbf{{Top}})
for the full subcategory spanned by the Noohi groups.

Noohi groups are useful when one wants to generalize Grothendieck’s Galois theory to allow infinite fibers (cf. the “infinite Galois theory” of
[
BS15
, §7.2]
).
This formalism was used to define the proétale fundamental group of a scheme in §7.4 of
loc. cit.
.
For any scheme
X
with locally finitely many irreducible components (this assumption suffices by
[
BS15
, Remark 7.3.11]
) and geometric point
\bar{x}\to X
, the group
\mathrm{\pi}^{\proet}_{1}(X,\bar{x})
is Noohi.
Similarly, the fundamental group of de Jong in rigid geometry
[
deJong:Etale_fund_grps_analytic_1995
]
and its later generalizations
[
ALY-variants
,
ALY23
]
are all Noohi.

Noohi groups can also be characterized in purely topological terms as Hausdorff, Raĭkov complete groups such that open subgroups form a fundamental system of neighborhoods of
1
.

The inclusion
\textbf{{Grp}}^{{\operatorname{Noohi}}}\subset\mathrm{Grp}(\textbf{{Top}})
admits a left adjoint
(-)^{\operatorname{Noohi}}
, called
Noohi completion
, given by

G\mapsto\Aut(F_{G})\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

See
[
LaraFESpaper
, §2]
for this and some other properties of Noohi groups and Noohi completion.

We now extend Noohi completion to condensed groups.

8.15 Definition
.

Let
G\smallin\Cond(\textbf{{Grp}})
.
The
Noohi completion
of
G
is the Noohi group

G^{\operatorname{Noohi}}\coloneq\left(G^{\mathrm{top}}\right)^{{\operatorname{Noohi}}}\hbox to0.0pt{\ {.}\hss}

8.16 Remark
.

For a condensed group
G
, one can also define a version of Noohi completion directly as a condensed group without passing through
(-)^{\mathrm{top}}
.
More precisely one can show that
\underline{G^{{\operatorname{Noohi}}}}
coincides with the condensed group defined by the assignment

S\mapsto\Aut\bigg\lparen\!\!\hbox to264.47pt{\vbox to23.08pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 132.23114pt\lower-11.54126pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
